Liverpool injury, suspension list and return dates for Crystal Palace: Ryan Gravenberch, Hugo Ekitike latest

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Nolan | 06 Nov 2026 03:58

Liverpool will look to respond to their latest defeat by beating Crystal Palace in the Premier League on Sunday at Selhurst Park.

The Reds not only lost 1-0 against Arsenal in the Premier League last weekend, but they also lost 3-2 against Fenerbahce on Wednesday in the Champions League.

Andoni Iraola cannot afford to head into the next international break on the back of a third consecutive loss in all competitions.

Here, Sports Mole rounds up Liverpool’s latest injury and suspension news ahead of their Premier League fixture against Crystal Palace.

Possible return date: November 8 (vs. Crystal Palace)

Ryan Gravenberch was left on the bench against Arsenal on November 1, and he was left out of the squad that faced Fenerbahce three days later.

Iraola confirmed that he was dealing with a fracture in his foot, and he has cast doubt on whether the Dutchman will be involved before the international break, though he has not ruled him out of featuring in some capacity against Palace.

Possible return date: November 22 (vs. Manchester United)

Speedster Jeremie Frimpong was brought on at half time against Brentford on October 17 but had to be replaced just seven minutes later with a hamstring injury.

The right-back is set to be sidelined until after the international break, and Iraola stated on October 19, "It’s going to be difficult, I think, before the [November international] break. We will try but it's going to be difficult to have him."

Possible return date: December 26 (vs. Hull City)

Striker Hugo Ekitike has been out of action since rupturing his Achilles against Paris Saint-Germain in April, and is going through rehabilitation following surgery on the tear.

The Frenchman is unlikely to take to the pitch for some time, but has been included in Liverpool's squad for the Champions League league-phase, and could return during the festive period.

Possible return date: January 2 (vs. Coventry City)

Nineteen-year-old centre-back Giovanni Leoni is another long-term absentee for Liverpool, having suffered an anterior cruciate ligament injury on his debut in September 2025.

Speaking on October 15, Iraola revealed that the defender has faced a setback, saying, "It’s taking more time than we wanted. The plan was to train with the team in this past international break [but] probably it’s going to be in the next [one]."

Possible return date: November 22 (vs. Manchester United)

Right-back Conor Bradley has been unavailable since picking up a major knee injury against Arsenal in January, though he is planning a return this autumn.

The academy graduate has yet to join team training, but is making progress in his rehabilitation and started doing ball work earlier this month.

Liverpool have no players suspended for this match.

XPENG Researchers and Academic Partners Introduce GroundingPI, An Open-Source Visual Perception Model For Physical AI

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Guangzhou, China — XPENG (NYSE: XPEV, HKEX: 9868), a leading Physical AI company, has supported the development of GroundingPI, an open-source visual perception foundation model created by researchers affiliated with XPENG, Peking University, The University of Hong Kong and other academic institutions.

The research explores a new approach to embodied intelligence by focusing on a fundamental capability: enabling AI systems to accurately identify objects and understand their spatial positions in the physical world. With approximately 4 billion parameters, GroundingPI achieved the highest aggregate score among 44 baseline models evaluated across 34 perception benchmarks covering 11 capability categories.

The research paper, model weights and supporting code have been made publicly available, enabling researchers and developers to further explore perception-focused approaches to Physical AI.

As AI increasingly moves beyond digital environments into physical applications such as robotics and autonomous driving, accurately understanding the surrounding environment becomes essential.

While general-purpose foundation models have demonstrated strong capabilities in language understanding, reasoning and planning, physical intelligence requires more precise visual perception. AI systems must not only recognize objects but also identify their exact locations and spatial relationships to support real-world interaction.

GroundingPI takes a perception-focused approach to this challenge. Rather than relying on robot action labels during the perceptual model’s training, the research focuses on developing foundational visual understanding capabilities that can support downstream action-learning systems.

For example, when instructed to identify “the handle of the cup on the right”, an AI system needs to locate not only the correct cup but also the precise position of its handle. GroundingPI is designed to represent such information using spatial coordinates, including points and bounding boxes.

The model is trained across 11 categories of perceptual capabilities, including object detection, dense object localization, referring expression grounding, optical character recognition (OCR), document layout understanding, user interface element recognition and robotic spatial localization.

By strengthening these fundamental capabilities, the research explores how specialized perception models could provide a more effective visual foundation for embodied AI systems.

In evaluations conducted by the research team, GroundingPI was compared with 44 baseline models across 34 public perception benchmarks spanning 11 capability categories.

Using an aggregate evaluation score with equal weighting across the capability categories, GroundingPI achieved 73.68, compared with 71.54 for GPT-6 Astra.

The results demonstrate the potential of specialized perception-focused models to outperform larger general-purpose models on specific visual grounding tasks. The comparison relates to the evaluated perception benchmarks and does not represent a broader assessment of general AI intelligence or reasoning capabilities.

GroundingPI’s performance was particularly relevant to tasks requiring precise object localization, including complex visual scenes containing numerous objects or small targets.

These findings suggest that targeted perceptual pretraining can provide a valuable foundation for future Physical AI research, complementing the broader reasoning and planning capabilities of general-purpose foundation models.

Beyond perception benchmarks, the research team evaluated GroundingPI as a visual foundation for downstream robotic manipulation and autonomous driving tasks.

The results highlight two potential advantages for embodied intelligence.

Robotic systems often require extensive human demonstrations to learn new manipulation tasks, creating significant challenges in data collection and training.

In RoboCasa-GR1 simulation experiments, robotic learning systems using GroundingPI as their visual backbone achieved higher task success rates with 50% of the available demonstration data than the evaluated comparison models trained with 75%.

This finding suggests that stronger foundational perception capabilities may reduce the amount of action-training data required for certain robotic tasks, potentially improving the efficiency of future embodied AI development.

Another major challenge in robotics is generalization: the ability to perform a learned task when objects, backgrounds or environmental conditions change.

In RoboTwin 2.0 simulation evaluations, GroundingPI demonstrated stronger performance than the evaluated mainstream visual backbones across four out-of-distribution settings, indicating improved adaptability to unfamiliar environments.

The research team also evaluated its potential for autonomous driving perception using the nuScenes dataset. When integrated as a visual backbone, GroundingPI achieved an average open-loop trajectory prediction error of 0.296 metres in the reported evaluation.

Together, these experimental findings highlight the potential of perception-focused foundation models to support more adaptable and data-efficient embodied intelligence systems.

The research remains exploratory. Its downstream results are based on simulation and offline evaluations and do not establish readiness for real-world commercial deployment.

GroundingPI explores a research direction in which specialized visual perception capabilities complement higher-level reasoning and planning, rather than relying exclusively on general-purpose models for every component of an embodied intelligence system.

The findings provide insights into how perception-focused pretraining could contribute to future research on robotic manipulation, autonomous driving and other applications requiring precise interaction with the physical world.

To encourage further exploration and collaboration, the research team has made GroundingPI’s paper, model weights and supporting code publicly accessible, subject to the applicable open-source and third-party licensing terms.

The project represents an academic research contribution supported by XPENG and its research collaborators. The results do not constitute an announcement of integration into XPENG’s commercial products or a commitment to future deployment.

Project Website: https://groundingpi.github.io/ Research Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39601 Source Code: https://github.com/groundingpi/GroundingPI Model Weights: https://huggingface.co/GroundingPI/GroundingPI

XPENG is a leading global Physical AI company, dedicated to bringing artificial intelligence into the physical world to reshape future mobility and smart living. Through in-house R&D, XPENG has developed a full-stack Physical AI architecture spanning Turing AI chips, world foundation models, and highly integrated software and hardware applications. This unified technology foundation of XPENG powers an expansive product portfolio of smart EVs, robotaxis, and humanoid robots, advancing the deployment of Physical AI at scale.

Headquartered in Guangzhou, China, XPENG is dual-primary listed on the New York Stock Exchange and the Hong Kong Stock Exchange. With global capabilities across R&D, manufacturing, sales, and services, XPENG drives continuous technological innovation and fosters an open Physical AI ecosystem, making life smarter, safer, and better for users worldwide. For more information, please visit https://www.xpeng.com/.

Ark Invest buys more Coinbase as shares plunge 10% in wider market rout

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Cathie Wood's Ark Invest bought $2.44 million worth of Coinbase, while selling $2.27 million in Bullish and $2.28 million in Bitmine shares.

Cathie Wood-led investment firm Ark Invest bought additional shares of Coinbase on Thursday but trimmed its holdings in Bullish and Bitmine amid a sharp sell-off in U.S. technology stocks.

According to its latest trading disclosure, Ark bought 17,806 shares of Coinbase, worth $2.44 million, through its Ark Innovation ETF on Thursday. The firm also sold 109,522 shares of Bullish, worth $2.27 million, and 119,841 Bitmine shares, worth $2.28 million, via its ETFs.

Ark's move came as major U.S. technology stocks plummeted in a market rout that erased post-election gains. The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite index closed down 5.52%, posting its worst day since April 2025, according to CNN. The S&P 500 lost 4.36%, and the Dow Jones Industrial Average dropped 3.44%.

Coinbase lost 9.69% to close at $136.82 on Thursday. Bullish shares fell 10.46%, while Bitmine slumped 12.56%.

AI-related equities were among the hardest hit, as Democrats' midterm election victory sparked fears of a potential slowdown in AI development. Nvidia shares fell 9.26%, wiping out their gains for the year. AMD plunged 17.26% but remained up 81% year-to-date. SpaceX dropped 9%.

Amid the sell-off, Ark purchased 252,592 Nvidia shares, worth $45.9 million, and 48,984 AMD shares, worth $19.8 million. The firm also added 393,774 SpaceX shares, worth $48.7 million, according to its trading disclosure.

The sell-off extended into Asian markets on Friday morning. South Korea's Kospi was down 3.6% around noon after plunging nearly 9% earlier in the day, triggering a brief trading halt. Japan's Nikkei 225 dropped 5.3% during the morning session.

Meanwhile, bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) traded down 5.8% over the past 24 hours at $75,908 as of 11:20 p.m. ET Thursday, after falling to around $75,350 in a local low earlier in the day. Ethereum Ethereum (ETH) dropped 8.2% to $2,234, according to The Block's crypto price page.

‘Lord of the Rings,’ ‘Avatar’ VFX House Weta FX Partners With Taiwan’s Moonshine Studio Under AI Pact With TAICCA

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Weta FX, the New Zealand visual effects company behind “The Lord of the Rings” and “Avatar,” is partnering with Taiwanese animation and VFX outfit Moonshine Studio.

The investment is part of a partnership with the Taiwan Creative Content Agency (TAICCA) aimed at making Taiwan an AI hub for Asia’s content industries.

Weta FX CEO Daniel Seah and TAICCA chair Sue Wang signed a memorandum of understanding on Friday at Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF) in Taipei. Taiwan’s Minister of Culture Li Yuan attended the signing. No figure was given for the investment. Related Stories Neon Partners With 'NAZA' Filmmakers, Guardian and MK2 to Bring Prize-Winning Doc to Wider Audiences Guillermo del Toro Tears Up as He Says ‘We’re Handing Down a World in Ruins’: ‘It Pains Me Deeply to See Three Generations Fail the Next’

Seah said the partnership has three parts. The first is the partnership with Moonshine, founded by Lin Chia-chi, which he said had been around six months in the making. The second is a technology platform that will invest in Taiwanese film and TV projects and use Weta FX’s global reach to get them in front of wider audiences.

“On the AI road, nobody is really far, far ahead,” Seah said. “We all have to venture into this tech-filled AI world together. That’s why, while investing in Taiwan, we’re investing in technology.”

The third part, and the one Seah said he values most, is talent. A program will allow Taiwanese filmmakers, particularly young ones, to come to Weta FX for exchanges and internships, and potentially to work there in future.

Seah has spent close to 16 years as a VFX CEO. That includes 14 years running Digital Domain, the company co-founded by James Cameron. He moved to Peter Jackson’s Weta FX nearly two years ago. Li noted that Seah was born in Malaysia and grew up in New Taipei’s Yonghe district before working in China and Hong Kong.

Weta FX’s staff alone, Seah said, make up around 2% of the population of Wellington, New Zealand’s capital.

“Over the past 30 years, Weta turned Wellington from a political capital into such an important global production base,” Seah said. “We think Taiwan has even more of that opportunity.”

Wang said Moonshine’s team ranked first among Taiwan’s VFX houses. “Every time we met the Moonshine team, my only worry was: Can you make money like this? How long can you keep going?” she said. Wang added that the team “deserves to be put to better use, and seen by more people.”

Wang also thanked Taiwan’s National Development Fund for its support.

Li summed up the day in an eight-character Chinese phrase, “Daniel Seah comes home, dreams come true.” The phrase plays on Moonshine’s Chinese name, which means “dream.” He said AI and cultural technology will be among the Ministry of Culture’s priorities for the next four years.

Li joined Wang Film Productions at 37 hoping to make original animation, and gave up two to three years later. “I thought maybe this dream would take 20 or 30 years to come true,” he said. “I didn’t expect that after waiting so long, I would at least see it come true with my own eyes.”

Also on hand were TAICCA president Erica Wang and three Weta FX executives: chief technology officer Kimball Thurston, VFX supervisor Sheldon Stopsack and R&D head Andrew Moffat.

Chicago Fire's Joe Minoso Addresses Cruz's Season 15 Exit After 300 Episodes

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Matthew brings more than a decade of experience writing about pop culture, movies, TV shows, wrestling, comic books, true crime, weather conditions, safety tips, taxes, wildlife, archaeology, music, and trending news. He is also an experienced interviewer with a strong track record of speaking with high-profile talent and subject-matter experts. He has conducted interviews with major actors, including Matt Damon, Matthew McConaughey, Bryan Cranston, Ethan Hawke, Jason Sudeikis, Jon Hamm, Alan Ritchson, Kumail Nanjiani, Rob McElhenney, Danny Trejo, Adewale Akinnuoye-Agbaje, Joshua Jackson, Damon Wayans, John C. McGinley, and Julia Jones. He has also interviewed several prominent TV personalities, including Howie Mandel, Ted Lasso star Phil Dunster, Happy’s Place star Rex Linn, the cast of Stuart Saves the Universe, and acclaimed series creators Bill Lawrence and Chuck Lorre. As a versatile entertainment journalist, he has also interviewed notable musicians such as Travis Barker and Nick Jonas, as well as sports icons like Olympic gold medalist wrestler Kurt Angle and NBA champion Jayson Tatum. Additionally, Matthew has interviewed reality show contestants from hit series like Survivor, Big Brother, and The Traitors. To round out his extensive portfolio, he has interviewed subject-matter experts on taxes, public safety, and winter weather conditions.

A beloved Chicago Fire star has addressed his exit from the show after being featured in 300 episodes of the series.

Since premiering on October 10, 2012, the drama series has reached its 15th season, which is an impressive feat. That also allowed the series to produce a landmark episode. And it was a massive draw as reviews of Chicago Fire episode 300 spread online as soon as it aired. Of course, much of that discussion centered on one thing: the fact that longtime series star Joe Miñoso left the show.

As viewers have been discussing Cruz's exit, Miñoso joined the discussion during a recent TVLine interview. While talking to the outlet, the popular actor was asked to explain what led to him leaving the show after so many years. In response, he noted that even though he didn't ask to be written off the show, he agreed with the creative decision. Check out Miñoso's comments below:

I will say, at the same time, there was something in me that felt it was time to move on. We had done so much with the character. I remember thinking about it a lot midseason [during Season 14], just because we had explored so much. And when we got the twins storyline, I was like, "This feels like the right time." So the news came my way, but I understood it, and they were so gracious.

After Cruz spent years as one of the most likable Chicago Fire characters, it is hard to see him go. However, that doesn't mean that the actor's association with the NBC drama is over. Instead, Miñoso told TVLine that he'd been asked to come back to direct episodes of the series.

Literally on the heels of that, I got asked if I was interested in still directing [episodes of the show], so it was real whiplash. And since then, it's been a journey into the world of this show in a really different way. I've had the great pleasure of shadowing a bunch of really amazing directors, and seeing what they do in that front office, which we don't get to see enough as performers. Those people are working their butts off so much every day, and it gave me more joy toward the process.

Anytime a longtime character leaves a show, observers typically focus on how they are written off. In Cruz's case, he was given a multi-episode arc focused on putting his family first after an injury and the birth of his twins. When asked about the storyline, Miñoso told the outlet how thankful he was for how his character's decision to leave the firehouse was handled.

I thought it was wonderful. We really haven't had the opportunity so much to see the dark soul moment for a character on this show, and seeing whether or not he can bring himself out of it. What's beautiful about it is of course it's all tied into the firehouse family and the hope that they bring him. I considered it an honor and a joy, and I thank them for trusting me with the material. That was some really intense stuff that they gave me to do, and I love them for it.

As anyone who has watched the ups and downs that Chicago Fire's characters have gone through can attest, the series has included some devastating moments. As it turns out, Cruz could have become the focus of another heartbreaking turn, as Miñoso told TVLine that his character's death was considered by the series' showrunner. Ultimately, however, the show's bosses thought that would be too sad.

While not going down that route saved viewers some despair, Miñoso told the outlet that leaving the show left him in tears for days. "I was crying for a week straight. And I thought I got a lot of that out of my system during the hiatus, but no, it was highly emotional."

For people who have watched Chicago Fire since the start, it will be strange to experience the show without Cruz. Still, as the One Chicago franchise has proven time and time again, one character disappearing is always a massive opportunity for other figures to step up. It will be fascinating to see what will happen now that a major hole has been left behind.

Chicago Fire airs Wednesdays at 9:00 p.m. ET/PT on NBC and streams the next day on Peacock.

Zac Affleck Says He ‘Begged’ Estranged Wife Jen to Stay Amid Divorce Filing

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Mormon Wives’ Zac Affleck is sharing more details after his wife, Jen Affleck, filed for divorce.

Posting via TikTok on Thursday, November 5, Zac, 30, spoke candidly about his side of the story, claiming that he “begged” Jen, 27, to give their marriage another shot and alleging he discovered that Jen had officially filed the papers via the media.

“I’m just going to speak from the heart,” Zac began. “Anyways, so yes, it is true that Jen filed for divorce yesterday, and it is true that I found out from a TMZ reporter about the filing, but regardless of that, this is something we’ve been talking about for a long time. Jen came to me about a year ago after Dancing with the Stars and told me she was unhappy and wanted a divorce, and I basically begged her to stay.”

Zac recalled promising to “work on things, fix things” and “become whoever she needed me to be.” However, he acknowledged the couple “ultimately just couldn’t make it work.”

Although the pair opted to go their separate ways, Zac praised his estranged wife and shared his hope that they can put their kids first moving forward. (Zac and Jen share daughters Nora, 5, and Penny, 16 months, and son Luca, 3. Jen is currently pregnant with the pair’s fourth child.)

“It sucks because I really do love Jen, and I still love Jen. And yeah, when I first met Jen, she had this light about her, unlike anyone I’ve ever met, and she really does have such a good heart,” he said. “I will continue to support her and be her biggest fan.”

Zac added that the worst part of the relationship breakdown for him was the potential impact on their children.

“I feel like I failed my kids and I’m worried that I’m going to cause them damage and I just hope that we can still give them a normal childhood,” Zac continued, adding that he and Jen are on the same page about wanting what’s best for them despite their split.

Zac went on to urge his followers not to pick sides in the break up, even though they are public figures.

“I don’t want anyone speaking bad about Jen, I don’t. I’ll just be the first to say I’ve made plenty of mistakes in our marriage, and it just really was time for us to go our own ways,” Zac said. “We got married so young, and it sucks because I really did feel like we had something pretty special when we got married, but life happened. We had four kids. We signed up for reality TV on a show that’s really hurt a lot of people’s lives.”

He continued, “It’s just hard. Marriage is hard. Having your marriage in the public is hard. Raising kids is hard, and I think we just, slowly over time, we lost that spark, and life happened. So I’m just coming on here to shut down any narratives.”

Jen filed a petition for divorce from Zac after seven years of marriage on Tuesday, November 3. Us confirmed on Wednesday, November 4, that she also submitted a proposed parenting plan, the details of which are private.

Mansfield Town vs Macclesfield Town - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

By Anthony Nolan | 06 Nov 2026 03:46, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 05:16

Arriving at One Call Stadium as underdogs, famous giant killers Macclesfield will face League One's Mansfield Town in the FA Cup's first round proper.

The Stags have only been eliminated at this stage in one of the last seven seasons, though the Silkmen are in strong form, despite their lower-division status.

Nigel Clough's Mansfield enjoyed their run to the FA Cup's fifth round in 2025-26, and they will be confident of kicking off their campaign in strong fashion this weekend.

That being said, the Stags have endured a mixed season so far, and head into this clash having won two, drawn two and lost one of their last five games.

Most recently, Clough's side were held to a 0-0 draw by relegation-threatened Wigan Athletic on Tuesday, marking the second consecutive match in which they have failed to score, following a 2-0 defeat to Notts County.

Those blanks have left Mansfield sitting 11th in League One with 19 points from 13 games, though a push for promotion is far from impossible, considering they are just three points behind sixth-placed Reading.

A run in the FA Cup could serve as a morale boost for a playoff charge, and supporters will be expecting to see the Stags progress at One Call Stadium, where they have lost just one of their six league outings this term.

Meanwhile, John Rooney's Macclesfield are best known for knocking out then-holders Crystal Palace in the FA Cup's third round last term, but they will need to navigate a pair of tough tests to make it through to face another Premier League club this time around.

This may be the first round proper, but the National League North side have already beaten Redcar Athletic 2-1, Scarborough 2-1 and Anstey Nomads 3-0 to reach this stage.

Those hard-fought victories have only served to bolster the Silkmen's resolve to prove they are more than a mere novelty, and having downed Bedford 3-0 last weekend - their fourth win in six games - the club will be cautiously optimistic.

That latest success marked a second clean sheet in three matches for Rooney's men, who boast the joint-best defensive record in the sixth tier, having conceded just 11 goals in 13 fixtures.

Macclesfield are also pushing for promotion in the National League North, where they are third with 27 points, a tally that has them just two off top-of-the-table South Shields.

However, the visitors remain significant underdogs, even if they have only tasted defeat once on the road across all competitions this season, winning six times and drawing twice.

Mansfield will be without centre-back Baily Cargill once again due to a hamstring injury, while Kyle Knoyle was forced off against Wigan through injury.

In their absence, Luke Bolton could start alongside Adedeji Oshilaja and Ryan Sweeney in the Stags' defence, flanked by Nathan Moriah-Welsh and Jordan Bowery.

Further forward, Michael Smith and Rhys Oates could lead the attack, platformed by a midfield featuring Louis Reed, George Maris and Elliott Hewitt.

As for Macclesfield, they are missing left-back Brandon Lee as he recovers from a long-term leg injury that has kept him sidelined since last season's FA Cup second round win over Slough in December 2025.

On Saturday, Cameron Borthwick-Jackson could operate on the left of a four-man defence, next to Tom Wilson, Sam Heathcote and Ammar Dyer.

Roberts; Bolton, Oshilaka, Sweeney; Reed; Moriah-Welsh, Maris, Hewitt, Bowery; Smith, Oates

Macclesfield Town possible starting lineup:

Dibble; Dyer, T Wilson, Heathcote, Borthwick-Jackson; Ustabasi, Shrimpton, G Wilson, Kay; Duffy, Elliott

Mansfield may have blanked in their last two matches, but they should have enough to get over the line against a sixth-tier opponent.

Macclesfield are one of the strongest sides in the National League North and could put up a fight, yet it would still come as a surprise to see anything other than a home win on Saturday.

Nintendo Reiterates Plans For Zelda's 40th Anniversary

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"We will pursue a variety of initiatives"

You can't really avoid it in the gaming space right now, but if you did somehow miss it, Nintendo is currently celebrating the 40th anniversary of the Zelda series.

Following the release of Ocarina of Time for the Switch 2 this week, the firm has now issued a reminder about the series' anniversary in its latest financial results briefing. For the remainder of this year and throughout next year, it will pursue a "variety of initiatives" to celebrate the series.

It's not only focused on game releases, and fans who cherish the "earlier titles", but also merchandise, music (a worldwide concert), and of course, the Zelda movie - scheduled to arrive early next year. Here's what's on the way:

"Over the course of this year and next, we will pursue a variety of initiatives to celebrate the 40th anniversary of The Legend of Zelda. In addition to games, we will expand opportunities for consumers to experience its world through the movie, music, character merchandise, and more.

"We will make the series even more appealing by offering different ways to enjoy it, both for those who cherish its earlier titles and for those discovering it for the first time."

Weta FX Partners With TAICCA To Position Taiwan As AI Hub; Invest In Moonshine Studio

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

New Zealand-based visual effects powerhouse Weta FX is striking a strategic partnership with Taiwan Creative Content Agency (TAICCA) to position Taiwan as an AI hub for Asia’s content industry.

The partnership involves investment into Taiwanese VFX house Moonshine Studio, founded by Chia Chi Lin. The Lord Of The Rings and Avatar VFX producer will also establish a joint venture platform to “identify exceptional Taiwanese work and help bring it to a technical level that can be on a par with international leading films and TV content”.

Weta and TAICCA will also work together through a Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) to develop AI technology and train new talent to use the tech to advance the Taiwanese content industry. Related Stories News Discover Asia's IPs & Co-Production Opportunities at Taiwan Creative Content Fest 2026 News AI Tools Bring Efficiencies But Have Limitations, Says Weta CTO Kimball Thurston: "Tech Companies Should Collaborate More With Creatives"

The MOU was signed at the opening of TCCF today by Weta FX CEO Daniel Seah and TAICCA Chair Sue Wang. Also in attendance were Weta Chief Technology Officer Kimball Thurston, Head of Research Lab, Technology & Research, Andrew Moffat and VFX Supervisor Sheldon Stopsack. Watch on Deadline

Seah grew up in Taiwan before studying law in Hong Kong and Beijing and working as an investment banker in Hong Kong and London. Wang described the deal as “one of our sons coming home”.

Seah said: “We have witnessed the thriving growth of the Hollywood industry and could see the great opportunities of the VFX industry. I’m very grateful that TAICCA chair Sue Wang has chosen me to come back to Taiwan to make my own contribution.

“We know that the AI revolution is an indistinguishable driving force for the content industry,” he continued. “And looking forward, we know that to create, we need to combine technology with artistry.”

Referencing support that Weta has received from the New Zealand government, Seah also said that “Taiwan is the home of creative talent and a semiconductor industry, so we need to utilise these strengths and that’s why this cooperation project came into being.”

Founded in 2012, Moonshine Studio has mostly worked on Taiwanese and Asian ads, movies and drama series, including action film 96 Minutes and drama series Gold Leaf/

Pam Abdy Laments Exits of Top Female Executives in Hollywood: “It Is Upsetting to See”

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Abdy, who was ousted as Warner Bros. co-film chief after Paramount Skydance completed its $111 billion acquisition of the studio, received Women in Film’s inaugural Vanguard Award on Thursday night.

By Mia Galuppo, Kirsten Chuba November 5, 2026 7:38pm

At Thursday night‘s Women in Film Honors, Pamela Abdy was on hand to receive one of the evening’s big awards, one month after being ousted as the film chief at Warner Bros.

“I have been relaxing and taking a breath and taking a minute and hanging out with my family,” Abdy told The Hollywood Reporter on the event‘s red carpet of what she has been up to in the past month, adding that her family was with her at the night‘s event, including her mother and daughter.

At this year‘s WIF Honors, Abdy is receiving the inaugural Vanguard Award, a recognition for a leader whose career embodies the qualities that move the industry forward, and is set to be presented the award by Nancy Meyers. Meyers worked with Abdy on her latest feature film, Paris Paramount, the beloved filmmaker‘s first time back in the director’s chair since 2015‘s The Intern. Related Stories Business Skydance Reveals Key TV and Streaming Leadership Roles Business George Cheeks Sets Leadership Team at Skydance TV

“It feels completely humbling,“ Abdy said of the recognition. “I am super proud to be a part of this organization and what it stands for and also be here to pass on whatever I can to the next generation. That is the most important thing to me.“

The honor was announced before her exit from Warner Bros., which took place as Paramount Skydance completed its $111 billion merger with Warner Bros. last month. During her tenure at the studio, Abdy oversaw a slate that included movies from female directors like Emerald Fennell, Maggie Gyllenhaal and Meyers; 2025 saw films directed by women drop to a seven-year low.

A subject that is being discussed with increasing alarm in industry circles, and was certainly a subject at Thursday night‘s WIF event, is the recent culling of top female executives during the ongoing layoffs and larger restructuring following the latest round of Hollywood mergers and acquisitions. In addition to Abdy, Cindy Holland exited her post as Paramount‘s streaming chief, while Bela Bajaria was ousted as Netflix’s chief content officer.

“Obviously, it is upsetting to see,“ Abdy offered of a raft of departures of female executives among Hollywood‘s top ranks. She went on to add: “But all of these women are incredible women and have incredible careers. These one moments don‘t define you. What defines you is the work you did and the work you left behind and the work you hope to do in the future. That‘s all we can keep doing, especially us executives, is try and support the next generation of storytellers and all of the artists.“

Where Abdy ends up next has been the subject of much speculation in Hollywood, with many wondering if she and her former Warner Bros. co-film head Mike De Luca would be taking over at Netflix’s film operation, after the firing of Dan Lin. When asked about her next move, Abdy said, “I have no idea what we are going to do next. Honestly, it is kind of a nice thing to not know.”

demi nguyen balances corporate branding, fintech systems, and typographic archives

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

demi nguyen translates complex ideas into visual identities, digital experiences, and communication systems, balancing strategic precision with an expressive approach to storytelling.

Han On edited by christina vergopoulou November 6, 2026 3 min

Based in Dallas, Texas, designer Demi Nguyen develops brand identities, digital interfaces, editorial publications, and communication systems across the technology, media, and cultural sectors. Informed by lived experience across Vietnam, Hungary, and the United States, her practice pairs structured research with experimental typography and modular graphic systems tailored to technical corporate contexts and cultural archives alike.

Within corporate environments, Nguyen works as a graphic designer in the global creative operations division at Texas Instruments, producing campaign graphics, enterprise collateral, and commemorative event assets such as visual identities for Jack Kilby Day. Her independent projects examine the intersection of digital systems and user accessibility: Kivo, a speculative fintech identity developed as a capstone, translates financial services into an approachable visual system tailored to creative entrepreneurs via AI platform prototypes, landing pages, and marketing toolkits. Similarly, for consultancy Team Union, Nguyen devised a modular design framework spanning competitor benchmarks, pitch decks, Figma proposal templates, and custom spot illustrations.

Her cultural and public communication portfolio encompasses broadcast and digital graphics for North Texas public media outlet KERA, as well as institutional print collateral, campus illustrations, and a 166-page annual report for Texas Christian University. Nguyen’s self-initiated research explores media translation and vernacular typography: Creator investigates YouTube digital culture through printed publication layouts, while Vietype functions as an open digital archive cataloging Vietnamese hand-drawn lettering and signage produced between the 1960s and 1980s. A complementary documentary photography practice captures light, domestic interiors, and streetscapes, reinforcing an observational methodology rooted in legibility, context, and structural coherence.

Ballon d’Or Harry Kane in trouble, three starters injured: Bayern Munich’s dirty evening

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

On paper, Bayern Munich had everything to experience a great celebration. This Saturday, during the Klassiker against Borussia Dortmund, the Bavarian club found its audience with a very special attraction: Harry Kane. Crowned Ballon d’Or 2026, the English striker presented his prestigious trophy in front of the Munich supporters, before returning to the field with the ambition to continue his momentum. But the scenario quickly turned into a nightmare for the Bavarians. Less inspired than usual, the former Tottenham striker did not manage to impact the match as much as hoped and even missed a penalty. A frustrating evening for the new best player in the world, who was particularly expected after his individual consecration. And as if that wasn’t enough, Bayern finally conceded the draw in the very last seconds of the match (2-2), letting slip away a victory that was within reach.

But beyond this frustrating result, it is above all the state of the infirmary which risks giving Bayern Munich headaches. In the space of a single match, the German club lost three starters to injury. The first to have been forced to leave his partners was Ismaël Saibari. After missing a one-on-one with the opposing goalkeeper, the Moroccan international tried to recover the ball, but collided with the goalkeeper. On this action, his knee and ankle suffered a worrying twist, forcing him to immediately give up his place. A few minutes later, Serge Gnabry found himself on the ground, visibly the victim of a muscular problem. Unable to continue the match, the German international also had to leave. And the bad news didn’t stop there since Michael Olise was also hit during the game before going out injured and requesting a change. Three major blows for the Bavarian offensive sector, while the severity of these injuries remains to be determined.

A real massacre which occurs in an already particularly delicate context for Bayern Munich. Because in addition to the infirmary filled with Luis Diaz, Manuel Neuer and Jamal Musiala, the Bavarian club still has to deal with the absence of its coach Vincent Kompany. Victim of illness last Friday at the training center, the Belgian technician was hospitalized for five days before being able to return home on Wednesday. Medical examinations revealed meningitis, which occurred after a viral infection contracted a few days earlier. Although his state of health has improved, the former Manchester City defender is still unable to return to his position on the bench. His English assistant Aaron Danks therefore continues to act on an interim basis, while Kompany continues his convalescence with rest and medical treatment. A necessarily unusual situation for Bavarian players, deprived of their head coach in a particularly busy period.

Between the unusual poor form of Harry Kane, the successive injuries to Saibari, Gnabry and Olise and this equalizer conceded at the last second, Bayern Munich will have experienced a particularly frustrating evening. The draw against Borussia Dortmund leaves a bitter taste. Already deprived of Vincent Kompany, the German club could now have to do without several important elements of its offensive animation. The next medical examinations will therefore be carefully scrutinized to determine the duration of unavailability of the three players. In the meantime, the Bavarians will have to quickly digest this evening and find solutions to prevent this accumulation of bad news from disrupting their season. Plus the Champions League is approaching…

Richard Garriott on the state of the games industry: 'The bells and whistles are no longer what's important'

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Ultima creator Richard Garriott was one of several developers on a recent panel hosted by the Georgia Game Developers Association, and Lord British was in fine form. Calling himself the "great-grandpa" of the group, he began by summarizing the current problems faced by the videogame industry.

"It's not just one thing," Garriott began. "I think if it was one thing it would be a lot easier to diagnose and come back from, but it's a lot of folks who don't understand videogames trying to make money off of videogames, it's a political climate, it's a billion things. All the threads have woven together into this horrible knot that we're seeing now."

He went on to describe a cycle he's seen play out multiple times over his years in the industry, where a technological advance comes along and everyone races to make a simple game that highlights that advance—whether it's the CD-ROM or a 3D-rendering pipeline or the internet—and only in the short gaps between these leaps forward is there much demand for something deeper.

"That cycle has been normal," Garriott said. "But we're at a new one, and this new one is machines are now fast enough to where the bells and whistles are no longer what's important." Without another advance having the same impact, he said, "we're at this kind of creative cliff where I think people don't know exactly what to do to prevent it and I think we've lost a lot of the original creators that were associated with those IPs and the bigger companies, and so I think we're floundering."

Garriott also had something to say about the way publishers have responded to the current state of the industry—by canceling games and laying off staff. Referring to his own publisher, Origin Systems, which he sold to EA in 1992, Garriott said, "Origin pre-EA, we never cancelled a project. Our theory was that if that team is capable of getting across the finish line, even if it doesn't sell well, they'll now know what it feels like to finish. And they will get feedback from the sales and the public as to what they did and didn't do right and so that team will be more strong, the individuals will be stronger, and we'll be able to circle back and do a more competent game next time.

"If you cancel a project, that means all those people on that team think you the publisher suck, because obviously their idea must've been brilliant, and so your willingness to cancel it was not their fault, but you the evil publisher. And half of them are probably going to leave or get fired, and maybe you'll scoop a few of them over into some other project that's now the priority project, but we thought that was a bad move on our own."

Things changed once EA snapped up Origin and took control, however. After that? "We canceled more projects than we completed by a long shot," Garriott said.

‘Pout-Pout Fish’ Studio Like a Photon Creative Unveils Seven-Film Animation Slate With Sola Media, Ahead of AFM (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Like a Photon Creative, the Australian animation studio behind “The Pout-Pout Fish,” has lined up seven new animated features and handed international distribution rights on the full slate to Sola Media.

Production finance comes from Elevate Production Finance and Alceon Entertainment Partners. Maslow Entertainment has picked up Australia and New Zealand distribution on the features. According to the companies, the lineup could create as many as 1,000 jobs in Australia and Ireland. Like a Photon Creative CEO Kristen Souvlis will attend the upcoming American Film Market (AFM). Related Stories Neon Partners With 'NAZA' Filmmakers, Guardian and MK2 to Bring Prize-Winning Doc to Wider Audiences Japan IP Market to Return to Cannes in 2027 as Tokyo Partnership Continues

First up is “Familiar,” an original developed in-house that will be made as a treaty co-production with Irish company Elephant Goldfish. Production on the film is expected to start in March 2027. Further titles will be revealed later.

The film is set at Ironclaw Academy, a school where the animal companions of sorcerers – known as familiars – go through a demanding and frequently messy training regime. Its central character is Josie, a feisty young cat.

“Families keep coming back to our films, and we’re focused on making projects that deserve that trust, with strong stories and broad appeal. ‘Familiar’ is the first of seven new features in our growing pipeline. Sola Media has a proven track record with our work, and together we see real opportunity to take Australian and European content to international audiences,” Souvlis said.

Alongside each of the seven features, the studio will make short-form direct-to-consumer material built from footage that won’t appear in the films themselves. That content will be bundled with the movies and rolled out across a range of online and social platforms.

The slate follows “The Pout-Pout Fish,” which Sola Media handled internationally. Adapted from the popular children’s book series, the film opened at No. 9 at the U.S. box office and currently ranks as the fourth highest-grossing Australian film of 2026. It also hit No. 1 on Amazon Prime Kids and No. 6 among all Prime viewers. Maslow released it in Australian cinemas on Jan. 1, with Viva Kids handling the U.S. release on March 20.

Sola Media will also oversee international distribution for the studio’s next release, “The Neverending Pillow Fort,” the first feature from Crayola Studios, produced with MIMO Studios. Allison Brownmoore directs the film, which is due in early 2027. Elizabeth Cullen and Sofia Rose voice best friends Maggie and Abby, with Guy Pearce, David Tennant, Teresa Palmer and Marsai Martin also in the cast.

Sola Media managing director Solveig Langeland said mixing humor and heart has always sat at the center of the studio’s storytelling, backed by its skill at bringing characters to life through strong voice casts. “We are proud of the successes we have achieved together and excited to embark on the next chapter of our partnership. With their expansion into co-productions with Ireland, Like a Photon is taking another exciting step forward. Their forward-thinking approach to building brands and engaging audiences through direct-to-consumer content from the earliest stages of production adds a powerful new dimension to their upcoming slate, creating awareness and marketing momentum long before the films reach cinemas,” she said.

Separately, Like a Photon Creative has teamed with Screen Queensland on the Escalate: Feature Focus Writers Initiative. Under the scheme, six screenwriters based in Queensland will each write a script for a possible 3D animated feature, working from concepts supplied by the studio. Writers’ rooms kick off in February 2027.

The studio has backed and made 10 animated features in 3D for worldwide theatrical release since 2017, among them “The Wishmas Tree,” “Combat Wombat” and its 2023 sequel, “Daisy Quokka: World’s Scariest Animal,” “The Sloth Lane” and “The Lost Tiger.” It also created the Sanctuary City multiverse for Universal Content Group.

Langeland founded Sola Media in 2004. The company, which has offices in Stuttgart and Munich, was acquired in 2020 by Embracer Group’s Plaion but continues to operate independently under Langeland and fellow managing director Moritz Peters.

Derrick Henry moves to seventh on the NFL's all-time rushing list

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Ravens haven’t had much to celebrate Thursday night, but Derrick Henry gave the home fans something to cheer.

Henry had 51 first-half rushing yards, which was enough to move him into seventh on the all-time rushing list. He passed Jerome Bettis (13,662) and LaDainian Tomlinson (13,684) in moving from ninth to seventh.

Curtis Martin is next on the list, with his 14,101 career rushing yards ranking sixth all time.

Henry entered the night with 632 yards for the season and 13,650 in his career.

Over the past four seasons, Henry has averaged 1,578 yards. He has averaged 85 yards per game in his career.

Henry will need to play another three to four years to break Emmitt Smith’s record of 18,355 yards.

Smith was 35 years old in his final season of 2004. Henry turns 33 in January.

Smith said earlier this year that he believes Henry has a chance to break his record.

As his memory slowly returns, he soon discovers he must solve the riddle behind a mysterious substance that's causing the sun to die out. As details of the mission unravel, he calls on his scientific training and sheer ingenuity — but he may not have to do it alone.

NYT Strands hints, answers for November 6, 2026

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Today's NYT Strands hints will be easy if you work like the outdoors.

Strands, the New York Times' elevated word-search game, requires the player to perform a twist on the classic word search. Words can be made from linked letters — up, down, left, right, or diagonal, but words can also change direction, resulting in quirky shapes and patterns. Every single letter in the grid will be part of an answer. There's always a theme linking every solution, along with the "spangram," a special, word or phrase that sums up that day's theme, and spans the entire grid horizontally or vertically.

By providing an opaque hint and not providing the word list, Strands creates a brain-teasing game that takes a little longer to play than its other games, like Wordle and Connections.

If you're feeling stuck or just don't have 10 or more minutes to figure out today's puzzle, we've got all the NYT Strands hints for today's puzzle you need to progress at your preferred pace.

These words are things found in a forest.

NYT Connections hints today: Clues, answers for November 6, 2026

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The NYT Connections puzzle today is not too difficult if you spend much time on the road.

Connections is the one of the most popular New York Times word games that's captured the public's attention. The game is all about finding the "common threads between words." And just like Wordle, Connections resets after midnight and each new set of words gets trickier and trickier—so we've served up some hints and tips to get you over the hurdle.

If you just want to be told today's puzzle, you can jump to the end of this article for today's Connections solution. But if you'd rather solve it yourself, keep reading for some clues, tips, and strategies to assist you.

The NYT's latest daily word game has become a social media hit. The Times credits associate puzzle editor Wyna Liu with helping to create the new word game and bringing it to the publications' Games section. Connections can be played on both web browsers and mobile devices and require players to group four words that share something in common.

Each puzzle features 16 words and each grouping of words is split into four categories. These sets could comprise of anything from book titles, software, country names, etc. Even though multiple words will seem like they fit together, there's only one correct answer.

If a player gets all four words in a set correct, those words are removed from the board. Guess wrong and it counts as a mistake—players get up to four mistakes until the game ends.

Players can also rearrange and shuffle the board to make spotting connections easier. Additionally, each group is color-coded with yellow being the easiest, followed by green, blue, and purple. Like Wordle, you can share the results with your friends on social media.

You Need A New Car? Go Electric! It’s Superior Technology. Part 2

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Some reasons why you shouldn’t be worried about buying an electric vehicle.

Everyone knows that electric cars are much more inconvenient than gas cars. After all, there is a gas station on every corner and it only takes 5 minutes to fill up. Electric chargers are rare, and it takes at least an hour to charge.

Wrong: If you have access to 220 volt electricity or a L2 charger at home or work, an electric vehicle is much more convenient than a gas car for 300 miles per day of local driving.

In principal: In the evening, you drive into your garage, carport or driveway and take 10 seconds to plug in, the next morning, you have a full charge and take 10 seconds to unplug.

Compare that with a gas car. You probably have to make a couple of special trips per week to the gas station, put in your credit card, grab the smelly nozzle, put it in your car, and wait five minutes to fill. Then you return the smelly nozzle to the pump.

With the gas car, every 3,000 miles, you need to go to a Jiffy Lube and sit in their smelly waiting room for an hour while they change your oil.

With the gas car, every year, you need to go to an inspection station and wait an hour in their smelly waiting room while they do an emissions check.

These three tasks are all eliminated with an electric vehicle, which makes it much more convenient for local driving.

How do you charge at home? Most electric cars come with an EVSE (Electric Vehicle Service Equipment) cable. If not, you can buy one for ~$200. On one end of the cable is the plug that goes into your car, and on the other end is an interchangeable plug. The one that comes with most cables is a standard 110V plug, but for $40 you can buy a 220V Nema 14-50 plug. Almost all RV parks have NEMA 14–50 sockets. If you have 220V service in your garage, you can have one installed for about $200. Northern Lights Electric installed one in my garage in Three Lakes Wisconsin for $130. An electrician in Saint George, Utah, installed one in our garage there for $250. It cost about $500 to have 220V service run to my garage in Lindon, UT. I had a L2 charger installed there for another $700, which I needed for my first Nissan LEAF in 2014. The L2 charger is not necessary if you have an EVSE, you only need a NEMA 14–50 outlet.

L2 charging gives you about 28 miles of range in one hour. Most EVs these days have more than 300 miles of range — so, in principle, you will charge from dead empty to full in ~11 hours. However, I am rarely at dead empty and I normally only charge to 80%, so usually I am only charging from one to five hours. Note: If you want your battery to last as long as possible, don’t charge over 80% or let the charge go below 20%. This is easier to do if you have a bigger battery.

L1 (110V) charging only gives you about 4 miles of range per hour. In principal, if you plug in next to your shaver in the bathroom, it would take three days for a full charge. However, if you drive fewer than 100 miles/day, this works.  We have friends who would need to upgrade the electrical service to the whole house to get 220V service to the garage, and they have gotten along fine with L1 charging. This works especially well since they have a second car. They also have a clothes dryer near the garage. They are able to use its 220V service or nearby Superchargers in special cases.

What if you live in a condo or other place where you can’t install your own charger? With considerable effort, my brother was able to get 220V service for L2 charging in the garage of his condo. If you have a L2 charger at work, it solves the problem. Also, there are many Superchargers in cities, and if you live near one, you can fill up there just as you would with a gas station.

The lowest price Tesla is the Model 3 RWD with seating for five, a range of 321 miles, at a price of $37,000 (see mine in the lead photo). That is plenty of range for both local and cross-country driving. However, if you want to up the range to 363 miles, you need to get the Model 3 Premium for $47,500. For only $3,000 more, you can get the base Model Y. It costs only $40,000. (See the photos below.) With the Model Y, you get a lot more room for baggage, you ride a little higher, you get a much bigger power liftgate, and you have the option of a factory-installed receiver/tow hitch, which is the one upgrade I chose because I often carry my ebikes on a rack in back. The standard Model Y seats five, but you can get the optional YL which seats 6 in three rows of captain chairs. I upgraded from my 2019 Model 3 to a base 2026 Model Y recently. You can get the premium Model Y with better external and internal styling and 363 miles of range for the RWD Model. It also comes with numerous other features, like faster charging, better audio speakers, and a small video screen for the rear seat passengers for another $5,000, as shown below. Four-wheel drive is a $2,000 option on all models and does reduce your range a little.

Every Tesla comes equipped with smart cruise control. Unfortunately, autosteer was eliminated recently unless you pay for Full Self Driving. If there is a car in front of you, you essentially have 100% self-driving until you need to make a turn. You have full automation in a traffic jam, where the car will move forward, automatically following the car in front of you.

I’m currently driving a base 2026 Model Y with FSD V14, which now costs $99/month. I can get into my car in my garage or a big parking lot or a Supercharger and without touching anything say, Hey Grok, then verbally say: Navigate me to [nearly any business, my home, or any address I dictate to the navigation]. Then FSD will drive me automatically out of my garage or the parking lot to the location requested and park my car in a parking lot or on the street (but not yet into my garage). I sit back and “supervise,” but seldom must intervene or complain about its actions as it drives.

“Tesla has introduced a new feature called Automatic Collision Evasion, which can autonomously activate Full Self-Driving (FSD) to avoid imminent collisions, even if the driver has not engaged the FSD system. This feature aims to enhance safety by allowing the vehicle to take evasive actions during critical situations.”

Note: In my opinion, Tesla customers that don’t opt in for the $99/month FSD subscription are missing a very important, possibly live saving safety feature.

There are literally 161 different new EV models to choose from in the US today. There are also several EV models that have been discontinued that you can buy used. If you can’t stomach buying a Tesla from Elon Musk’s company, you can choose one of those.

I will present two good alternatives to buying a Tesla: The top selling EVs in the US in 2026 to date right now are as follows. You can see that the Teslas are by far the most sold, but the Hyundai IONIQ 5 and the Toyota bz are reasonable alternatives for Musk haters.

Toyota led the movement towards automobiles that are more friendly to the environment with the Prius over 20 years ago. However, they were very slow to offer a compelling pure battery electric vehicle. That has now changed, Toyota’s bz EV has received excellent reviews. I had been a big customer of Toyota gas cars until recently, so I dropped down to the nearest Toyota dealership to see the status of EVs there, as shown in the photos below. I was pleased that the dealer had four bzs in stock and I took one for a quick spin. It had the usual excellent Toyota workmanship, with spectacular acceleration like all EVs. However, the driving automation, like in the Corolla I had rented in the spring, is lame. The lane assist will only work on gentle turns.

If you find any of my articles helpful to you, please use my referral link when buying a new Tesla: https://ts.la/arthur73734 (Be sure to use it when you make your order). Tesla keeps changing the program, but here’s the latest: If you are buying a new Tesla Model 3 or Y and use my link, you will get two months of free Full Self Driving (a ~$200 value) on top of the one month that comes free with every new Model Y right now. This will get you a total of 3 months free Full Self Driving. It will be $99/month after that. For a Cybertruck, you will get $1000 off your purchase price. At this moment, Tesla is offering a year of free Supercharging with the purchase of a new Model 3 Premium or Performance.

'High Potential' Officially Won't Return Until 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

ABC is running with a bit of a lighter schedule this fall. R.J. Decker has already wrapped up its short seven-episode sophomore season, leaving just 9-1-1, 9-1-1: Nashville, Grey's Anatomy, and the comedy duo of Scrubs and Abbott Elementary among its scripted programming. The Disney-owned network has some of its biggest series saved for the midseason, including new seasons of The Rookie and Will Trent, which have been regular fixtures in the spring, as well as buzzy newcomers The Rookie: North and an untitled Grey's spin-off. Easily the most surprising move, however, was the decision to hold back High Potential.

Since bursting onto the scene in 2024, It's Always Sunny in Philadelphia star Kaitlin Olson has been one of network television's most popular detectives. After ruling all of broadcast television in viewers in the 2024-25 season, Morgan Gillory's sophomore outing as an unlikely crime solver ended as the far and away leader in the key 18-49 demographic, going neck-and-neck with CBS' juggernaut one-two punch of Tracker and Marshals overall, with 16 million viewers tuning in over 35 days across all platforms. Despite that, the network ultimately decided to shake up the formula, meaning no new episodes until 2027. It's not an unusual move, considering how The Rookie and Will Trent have fared, but it is nonetheless a change of pace that will have viewers waiting until after the Super Bowl for the next case.

The good news is that the wait won't be long after the Lombardi Trophy is handed out for High Potential Season 3. ABC's megahit procedural will air a special episode right after Super Bowl LXI concludes on Sunday, February 14. While the network hasn't unveiled its entire midseason schedule, that time slot will put an especially massive spotlight on the series coming off a Season 2 finale with a lot of loose ends. Ultimately, the extra wait might be for the best, considering High Potential is undergoing a bit of a creative overhaul. Nora and Lilla Zuckerman, who previously worked on Rian Johnson's Poker Face and were tapped to co-write Scream 8, officially took over as showrunners following Todd Harthan's exit.

The show that claimed the most of your answers is the world you were built for. If two tied, both are shown — you're complicated enough to straddle two Sheridan universes.

You are a Dutton — or you might as well be. You understand that some things are worth protecting at any cost, and that the modern world's indifference to history, to land, to legacy, is not something you're willing to accept quietly. You lead from the front, you carry your family's weight without complaint, and when someone threatens what's yours, you don't escalate — you finish it. You're not cruel. But you are absolute. In Yellowstone's world, that combination of ferocity and loyalty doesn't make you a villain. It makes you the only thing standing between everything that matters and everyone who wants to take it.

You thrive in the chaos of high-stakes negotiation, where the money is enormous, the margins are thin, and the wrong word in the wrong room can cost everyone everything. You're a fixer — the person called when a situation is already on fire and needs someone with the nerve to walk into it. West Texas oil country rewards exactly what you are: sharp, adaptable, unsentimental, and absolutely clear-eyed about what people want and what they'll do to get it. You're not naive enough to think this world is fair. You're smart enough to be the one deciding who it's fair to.

You are a Dwight Manfredi — someone who has served their time, paid their dues, and arrived somewhere unexpected with nothing but their reputation and their wits. You adapt without losing yourself. You build loyalty through respect rather than fear, though you're not above reminding people that the two aren't mutually exclusive. Tulsa King is for people who are still standing when everyone assumed they'd be finished — who find, in an unfamiliar place, that they're more capable than the world gave them credit for. You don't need a throne. You build one, wherever you happen to land.

You carry the weight of a system that is broken by design, and you do it anyway — because someone has to, and because you're the only one positioned to do it without the whole thing collapsing. Mike McLusky's world is for people who are comfortable operating where there are no good options, only less catastrophic ones. You speak every language: law enforcement, criminal, political, human. That fluency makes you invaluable and it makes you a target. You've made your peace with both. Mayor of Kingstown belongs to people who understand that keeping the peace is not the same as being at peace — and who do the job regardless.

Created by Drew Goddard, High Potential has followed Morgan as she's risen from an LAPD cleaner with an IQ of 160 to a civilian consultant and recognized member of the team for her ability to unconventionally solve the most complex cases. Her approach has made her the perfect partner for the otherwise by-the-books Adam Karadec (Daniel Sunjata), whom she's grown closer and closer to throughout her work. Season 2 ended on a particularly bloody note, with Steve Howey's Captain Wagner being left for dead after a brutal stabbing. His fate is up in the air, but whether he survives or not, the actor will be stepping back for this season. The appearance of a shadowy figure at Ava's art show also left everyone asking if Roman had actually made an appearance and if he'd finally properly show his face in Season 3.

High Potential returns early next year following the Super Bowl. Stay tuned here at Collider for more details as it draws closer.

Te Araroa day 10: My first zero day

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

0km, Amelia and Nathan’s house in Kerikeri

It was delightful to wake up in a bed – a double bed no less. I think the last time I slept in a double bed was in Vietnam in March! The sleep-out isn’t fully finished so there weren’t any curtains in my room, which was fine by me. I woke up before the sunrise anyway. The others had said they wanted a slow morning, so I spent quite a while in bed writing and uploading blog posts before anyone else was up. I also called my parents, who I hadn’t spoken to since before I started hiking. We had bought stuff for a shared breakfast the night before but eventually I was too hungry and couldn’t wait anymore, and ended up grabbing some toast.

When the others were up, we made breakfast and had a guided tour around Amelia and Nathan’s large garden. Seeing Nicole’s reaction to the house and garden remided me that I felt the same when I first arrived in NZ. I was really taken aback by the amount of land a lot of people have over here. In our small, densely populated bits of Europe (Nicole is Swiss) land is very expensive, and you would assume anyone with a garden that size was rather wealthy. But over here, I guess land is relatively cheap (outside of the main population centres anyway). So having big gardens or a paddock or two is much more common.

Then we all spent some time trying to plan the next section. Ruby was trying desperately to organise kayaking from Paihia to Waikare, but the person who would drive the kayaks back was away until Thursday. Nicole and I were trying to figure out how many days of food we would need, and where to stay. It was a challenge because the accommodation options aren’t all shown on the map, some are only in the ‘accomodation’ section of the trail notes, and some are even buried in the trail description. We were getting a bit frustrated at the lack of clarity from the TA app. Based on the mileage though, it was clear it would be quite a long stretch to the next ‘proper’ supermarket, a Four Square in Ruakaka. Four Squares are small local supermarkets usually found in smaller towns in NZ. They can be highly variable in terms of how big they are and what they sell, but TA hikers have to rely on them for resupply in quite a few places. After Paihia, we wouldn’t be hitting another big supermarket until Mangawhai, in over 200km.

Eventually we admitted defeat and decided to take about 5 days of food and plan the rest on the fly. There are enough small shops and dairies in between that we figured we would make it work. It was getting on for lunchtime and we needed to go to the gear shop and the supermarket.Ruby drove us into town and we went into Hunting and Fishing. I got a new headlamp, and I think Ruby needed gas. Then we headed across the road to Woolworths. I managed to get most of the things I wanted, although I find the Woolworths bulk section very lacking compared to New World and Pak’N Save. I got some food for lunch too, and Nicole and I bought some alcohol for Amelia and Nathan as a thank you.

We picked Zoe up in town, where she had been staying with some friends of friends, and headed down towards the Stone Store where we had finished our hike the previous day. Nicole had seen a slice she wanted but hadn’t been able to get it as the cafe was closing. We had a nice lunch by the river, then dropped Zoe off and headed ‘home’ to organise our resupplies.I was quite pleased with the amount of food I had. Actually, I started wondering if I should have got more!

Later in the afternoon, the others all went for a swim but I wanted to continue doing my blog posts. I’d been so far behind and it was feeling good to catch up. I got a couple of posts done by the time the others came back, and when they were done showering we headed over to the main house to make pizzas. It was a nice evening and cool to get to know Amelia and Nathan a bit – and hear some of their stories from outdoor adventures with Ruby and Matt! After dinner I spoke to a friend on the phone, and eventually went to bed.
