You Need A New Car? Go Electric! It’s Superior Technology! Part 1

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Your car is getting “long in tooth,” or maybe your daughter is going away to college and you are giving her your car. Whatever the reason, you are looking for another car. It could be new, it could be used: don’t even look at gas cars, electric cars are superior technology!

The wheels on every railroad locomotive in the world are driven by electric motors. All the busiest rail routes in Europe and China are pure electric, including high-speed rail. 98% of new cars in Norway are electric, over 80% of new cars in Denmark are electric. In China, the country with the largest sales of new cars in the world, the percentage of new electric cars in September 2026 was 67%. 82% of the operating bus fleet in China is electric. Even ships and airplanes are going electric. The data is unequivocal: electric propulsion is superior to internal combustion propulsion. Only the huge political contributions to Trump by the fossil fuel industry and his resulting anti-EV policies have slowed down adoption in this country.

In places we’ve been like California; Portland, Oregon; and northern Utah, you are seeing a lot of Teslas, so you know that electric cars are working for a lot of people these days. It’s time for you to do some research on electric cars.

You do pay a premium for new electric cars, because the batteries are still quite expensive. If price is the driving factor for you, buy a used electric car. Hardly any used EVs are high mileage, and EV batteries still have plenty of life even after they go off warranty at 8 years and 120,000 miles. There are thousands of lightly used EVs sitting at used car lots at reasonable prices all over the country these days. Some used car dealers will give you an estimate of the battery life. If not, get a friend who is an experienced EV driver to help you evaluate the battery life of the EV you are considering.

If you just do local driving or have two cars and have a garage or off-street parking, an EV is a no brainer. EVs are hands-down more convenient than gas cars for local driving. Your EV will have plenty of range for local driving, it will get a complete charge overnight in your home garage refueling station and it will never need an oil change.

EVs are as quiet as mice, as smooth as kittens, and as quick as jackrabbits. They have simple electric motors, not complex internal combustion engines. They have simple one-speed transmissions, not complex 7-speed automatic transmissions. They don’t have exhaust systems with mufflers that will rust and need replacement. They don’t have emission control systems with limited life. They don’t have complex cooling systems with big radiators. You seldom use your brakes with an EV, so your brakes may last the life of the car. They don’t have a big lead-acid battery for starting, with a maximum life of 5 years. New EVs have only a small lithium-ion battery, which lasts 10+ years. Bottom line: Routine maintenance for EVs is almost nil. I think the only real maintenance item on a Tesla over the first 3 years is the cabin air filter. You can expect an EV to go 500,000 miles without major repair or replacement in most cases.

Perhaps you can stomach buying a car from Tesla whose CEO has become one of the most despicable persons on the planet lately. (Example: Musk recently suspended access to his Starlink communications system to the Ukrainians who are valiantly defending their country from the Russian aggressors.) However, Tesla has sold almost 10 million automobiles worldwide that don’t emit CO2 and particulate pollution while operating. Tesla also has by far the most ubiquitous charging system, which allows cross-country driving on major US roads with little planning. Consider buying a Tesla.

So, what about cross-country trips? Everyone knows that long-distance trips in an electric car are inconvenient or even impossible, because there aren’t enough charging stations and it takes an hour if you can find one. Wrong: Every 100 miles along all the major Interstate highways, Tesla has built Superchargers with 8 to 32 and a few with over 100 charging stalls. By the time you have gone to the bathroom, walked the dog, and chatted with a fellow EV owner for a few minutes, you probably have more than enough charge to continue your journey. Usually, charging takes about 20 minutes. If you need a longer charge, you may need to watch a bit of Octopus Teacher or Back to the Future II on the big screen in the car for another 10 minutes.

News flash! Since February 28, 2023, most Tesla Superchargers (now North American Charging Standard/NACS chargers) are usable by all EV brands. Unfortunately, older Tesla Superchargers can’t be used by other brands, so I would still advise buying a Tesla if you want carefree cross-country driving. CCS chargers which are native to most other brands have sprung up by the thousands in the US, which together with the newer Tesla Superchargers make it possible to travel cross-country with non-Teslas on many routes with some planning. Also, Teslas can use non-Tesla chargers. Non-Tesla chargers often come with Tesla cables now, so Tesla owners don’t even need an adapter in most cases. However, even Teslas require careful planning if you are driving far from major highways.

My wife and I have done cross-country trips from Utah to Wisconsin for 7 years, on to North Carolina (once), and back to Utah. We have also traveled from Utah to Southern California at the same 500 miles per day rate that we used to do with our gas cars … and with no worries, mate.

I’m going to try to pass on some things I’ve learned from driving five different electric vehicles over 12 years and some 200,000 miles. See part two, coming soon.

This will get you a total of 3 months free Full Self Driving. It will be $99/month after that. For a Cybertruck, you will get $1000 off your purchase price. At this moment, Tesla is offering a year of free Supercharging with the purchase of a new Model 3 Premium or Performance.

Nile Rodgers Defends AI Use in Music Video: ‘We Created Everything … The AI Did What We Told It to Do’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Nile Rodgers recently shared a snippet of his upcoming video for a new version of “The Christmas Song,” his 2020 charity collaboration with Rebecca Ferguson, proudly revealing it used AI, which fans took issue with. On Thursday, he defended his use of the technology.

Earlier this week, he shared an “AI capture” of the project. “#working on a #great #art project that’s next #level. This is the start of the process. This AI capture was very thorough and I’m so excited about the finished project,” he wrote. “When I saw the Eagles at The Sphere in Vegas I envisioned stories with incredible environments & music. It’s future time!”

Shortly afterward, however, people began commenting on their disappointment with him using AI. One person wrote in the IG comments, “There’s better way to preserve your legacy.” Rodgers responded, “Wait to you see it before you make judgement. You’re imagining the worst instead of imagining the best.” The person responded back: “I don’t have to imagine my friend, look what AI has done to art in general yet alone music. Promoting it in such a big way is not the way to go.”

On Wednesday, Rodgers shared photo stills from the project, and the backlash continued. “We need happiness and healing in our world,” he captioned the carousel. “When I had my 1st bought of #cancer it was a child who coached me and gave me #courage to fight. I know a lot of children who are bedridden and ill. Their main form of entertainment is the iPad or other such devices. I produced ‘The Christmas Song’ to give them a little #joy during the holiday season.”He added: “We gave the #video a fun twist on the story line…and in the process expanded a new creative family for me. Over the next few weeks while we finish this video I’ll show bits and pieces while we rush to get it ready by November 20. Peace, love, and good health for the holidays.” Trending Stories Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’ Nicki Minaj Is Going Down With Trump’s Ship After Nearly Every Candidate She Backed Lost As Nicki Minaj Rallied for Trump's GOP, Her Lawyers Told Court She Still Owes Them $295,000 Adam Lambert on His Future With Queen, New Music, and Why the Show Must Go On

Several commenters on his post from Wednesday took him to task for using generative AI as well. Rodgers defended its use, telling one commenter: “We created everything. Mood boards, story boards and did it the same way we’d do a film. The AI did what we told it to do and we painstakingly created every second you’ll see on the screen… If this were a traditional film it would’ve cost about $100,000.”

Rodgers told another commenter that “it’s somewhat hypocritical to say you’re anti AI and you’re using it all the time because it makes your life convenient,” he wrote. “I used to work with Microsoft and Apple. I’m very aware of the things they do when they have new tech that consumers like yourself adopt its usage. Love to you. I respect all opinions.”Rodgers has previously shown support for AI. In 2024, he told Euronews, “So, I think using AI-generated vocals to copy another person’s sound is horrible, but using AI to artistically express yourself and make better music, I think, is fantastic. The way I want to look at AI is from the positive side. I want to look at it as just another color on your palette,” he said, likening it to other tools. He cited how when drum machines and synthesizers first came out, people were saying it “wasn’t real music,” and how auto-tune helped shape the pop landscape. “I think that creativity comes from the artist. Now, if you have a tool to help you become more creative, there’s nothing wrong with that.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Dropbox acquires Tigris Data for $350M, releases new AI productivity features

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Dropbox Inc. is acquiring cloud storage startup Tigris Data Inc. for $350 million.

The company announced the deal today in conjunction with a major update to its product portfolio. It introduced DBX Agent Cloud, a service that artificial intelligence agents can use to store their data. Dropbox is also adding new AI productivity features to its flagship file sharing platform.

Sunnyvale, California-based Tigris Data offers an object storage service that runs across multiple public clouds. The company says that it enables applications to read and write several times faster than competing offerings. Furthermore, the service includes several features designed to optimize AI workloads.

AI agents often run in sandboxes that include, among other resources, the data they need to carry out their work. Loading data into a sandbox can be a relatively time-consuming process, which slows agent performance. Tigris Data offers a feature called bucket forms that speeds up the workflow. Furthermore, it prevents agents from modifying information that is used by other workloads.

Tigris Data’s service monitors the locations from which agents access records. When it detects a new location, it moves a copy of the records to a nearby server in order to reduce latency. The service caches the most frequently used files on high-speed storage infrastructure to further reduce access times.

Dropbox said Tigris Data will support its push into the AI infrastructure market. The company debuted its first entry into that product category, DBX Agent Cloud, in conjunction with the acquisition announcement. Like Tigris Data’s service, it’s an S3-compatible object storage offering optimized for AI agents.

The storage stalwart plans to add a file system capability further down the line. Object storage is best suited for unstructured datasets, while file systems are used to store records that are organized into folders. Dropbox plans to release the technology alongside “content processing capabilities.” The company says that those features will make it easier for AI models to process information kept in DBX Agent Cloud.

The offering is rolling out alongside a major overhaul of Dropbox’s flagship file sharing platform. An enhanced search bar will enable users to look for images by entering a description of the file they seek.

Furthermore, Dropbox can now respond to questions about a file by generating a natural language summary. A worker could, for example, request an overview of the changes that colleagues have made to an important folder over the past week.

Dropbox can not only edit questions about files but also edit them. The company says that users can have the built-in AI models change a file’s format, move it to a different folder or combine it with other records into a PDF document. Several other actions are supported as well. Users can mix and match actions into multistep workflows.

The new AI productivity features are initially available to customers in the U.S., the U.K. and Australia with Standard and Advanced subscriptions. Dropbox plans to expand access next year.

Amy Winehouse’s ‘Back to Black’ Reissue Sparks Top 10 Chart Return

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The late singer's sophomore set makes a top 10 splash on four charts.

Amy Winehouse’s Grammy-winning Back to Black album impacts the top 10 of four Billboard charts, following its 20th-anniversary reissue. The album re-enters the top 10 of Indie Store Album Sales (No. 3), Vinyl Albums (No. 4), Top Album Sales (No. 7) and Top R&B Albums (No. 7) (all charts dated Nov. 7). The project also jumps back onto the overall all-genre Billboard 200 at No. 42.

Back to Black peaked at No. 2 on the Billboard 200 in 2008, following its big night at that year’s Grammy Awards, where Winehouse won record of the year, song of the year, best female pop vocal performance (for the album’s “Rehab”), best new artist and best pop vocal album (for Back to Black).

For its 20th anniversary, the album was reissued in a variety of options on Oct. 23, including an expanded version with previously unreleased b-sides, demos and a live concert.

Back to Black marked the second and final studio album from Winehouse before her death in 2011, and spun off the Billboard Hot 100-charting singles “You Know I’m No Good” and the No. 9 hit “Rehab.”

In the tracking week ending Oct. 29, Back to Black earned 16,000 equivalent album units (up 185%) in the United States, according to Luminate. Of that figure, 11,000 was in pure album sales (up 885%), with the remainder driven by streaming activity and track sales. (All versions of the album, old and new, are combined for tracking and charting purposes.)

Back to Black is among seven debuts or reentries in the top 10 of the latest Top Album Sales chart. The highest debut of the week belongs to Anderson .Paak and Cordae’s collaborative project Heavy Is the Crown (No. 2), while My Chemical Romance’s The Black Parade jumps 29-1 following its own 20th anniversary reissue.

"You Don't Want To Interrupt The Player's Experience" - Here's Why Zelda's Switch 2 Remake Cut Some Mario References

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Nintendo is known in recent times for dropping Star Fox (and even Pikmin) into the Super Mario universe, but with the Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake on the Switch 2, it decided to take a little more care.

In the castle courtyard, where Link meets with Zelda, you might have noticed how the character portraits for Mario and friends are not in the Switch 2 version of the game. If you are wondering about the thought process behind this, Zelda series producer Eiji Aonuma has explained how the team didn't want to break the immersion.

Here's what he had to say during the same chat with Game Informer:

Eiji Aonuma: "Certainly, at the time, when we put those [portraits] there, I don't think anybody thought it was too big a deal. Maybe there was a more carefree or comfortable approach to that sort of thing. But looking at it now, like if a player is coming through that area, and given the overall way that we're, you know, creating this world and giving the player that form of expression, if they look up and they think, "Why is Yoshi there?” We don't want that moment to interrupt their experience of the world in that way. So we're feeling a little bit differently about it this time."

If you recall, the 3DS game also had Skyward Sword art hidden in certain locations, and this is also gone from the Switch 2 remake. According to Aonuma, the same reasoning applies here - the team didn't want to "interrupt the player's experience by seeing something that's unrelated to what they're currently doing in the game".

Although these easter eggs are gone, certain references to the Mario series remain intact as they are part of the character designs:

"You will remember that there is one design element on Talon with Bowser on it, and that's part of the original design of the character. So we didn't change that. That stays as is."

There’s no reason they need to treat an all-time classic as a museum piece, because the original game will always be a masterpiece. Better to iterate upon it with fresh ideas and convenient tweaks than to declare any changes off-limits because of Nostalgia™.

WhatsApp’s AI-powered photo expansion tool starts rolling out on iOS

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Select WhatsApp users on TestFlight and in the main app are now seeing the option to expand an image in the app’s photo editor. Here are the details.

iPhone users with access to Apple Intelligence have a native AI-powered photo expansion tool called Extend, which lets users pinch or drag to expand an image beyond its original borders.

This feature, which is also present on AI chatbots such as ChatGPT and image-editing platforms like Photoshop, is now also making its way into WhatsApp.

According to WABetaInfo, the platform is rolling out a Meta AI-powered image expansion tool, though it is currently limited to select users, primarily those in WhatsApp’s beta program via TestFlight.

When users select a photo in their chats, WhatsApp opens the drawing editor. Here, they can choose to apply some AI effect to improve it. Among these, users can find a new option to expand their photos. Once users select this option, Meta AI takes a few seconds to analyze the photo and generate the expanded version. Once the process is complete, WhatsApp shows the expanded photo in the drawing editor, and users can choose to send it. Note that this feature is also compatible with status updates.

The site notes that if the user isn’t happy with the result, they can tap an undo button to revert to the original and then try using the tool again to generate a different result.

As is usually the case with beta features on WhatsApp, there is no telling when it might become widely available.

That said, given that it has also rolled out to users in the Android beta and some users on the public iOS version already have access, a broader rollout might not be too far off.

How Bobby Pulido Went From Latin Grammy-Winning Tejano Singer to Congressional Elect

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Even before winning his highly contested congressional race this week, Bobby Pulido had made up his mind to give up music and focus on public service. “If people want me to represent them and they believe that I could best represent them, I’m perfectly comfortable never having a concert again,” he told Rolling Stone last month. Then he admitted bashfully, “I’m putting a karaoke machine in my house.”

Pulido said it in jest, but there’s probably some sadness as he leaves his music days behind him. After all, that’s a 30-year career as a singer he’s saying goodbye to. Pulido was born in Texas, the oldest to Tejano musicians Robert Pulido and Diana Montez, each who carved out their own respective paths in the genre. As a kid, Pulido played music with his high school mariachi and ended up in his dad’s band Los Clásicos. One of his first songs ended up being “Contigo,” a collaboration with his dad from 1994.

That song pretty much kick-started an impressive run for Pulido. The next year, he released his debut album Desvelado, spawning off his first hit “No Se Por Que,” which made its way onto Billboard’s Latin charts. The title track “Desvelado” also saw major success and remains a standard in Pulido’s catalogue. For a long time, fans thought the song was originally written for Selena Quintanilla, though Pulido dispelled the rumors in an interview.

Pulido had momentum and kept going. He followed his debut up with Enséñame, an album that blended cumbia and ranchera sounds with country sensibilities, and Llegaste a Mi Vida, another success on Billboard’s Regional Mexican Albums chart. Zona de Peligro came in 2000, followed by Siempre Pensando En Ti in 2001. Though those albums fell in the years Tejano music slowed down, Pulido picked things back up with Bobby, landing a hit with “Vanidosa.” Pulido stayed busy with more albums and a few featured roles on several telenovelas. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Though Pulido’s later albums saw less commercial success, the 2020s brought some major breaks: He won his first Latin Grammy in 2022 for Best Tejano Album after the release of his record Para Que Baile Mi Pueblo. Even when he announced he was stepping away from music to pursue politics, the musical success kept coming: a second Latin Grammy came in 2025, this time for Best Tejano Album for his album, Bobby Pulido & Friends: Una Tuya y Una Mía (Vol. 1/En Vivo). He also scored a nomination at the Grammys for Best Mexican Music Album (Including Tejano) for Bobby Pulido & Friends: Una Tuya y Una Mía (Por La Puerta Grande). The second part of that album is up for a Latin Grammy at the ceremony, which will take place on Nov. 12.

Pulido announced he was running and gave an exclusive interview to Rolling Stone at the time. When asked what he’d say to people who doubted he could run an effective political career because of his music, he responded, “I don’t blame them. I have been incredibly careful not to mix politics with my career. I understand if somebody says, ‘What does he know? He’s a singer.’ But that’s why you talk about the issues.” He also evoked the president’s career in pop culture. ” If you voted for the guy that was a reality TV star, and if you voted for the woman that is now our congresswoman that was selling insurance out of a strip mall, did you ask her the same question?”

Throughout his campaign, Pulido kept his connection to music strong. The campaign put out a public call play at local quinceañeras, which are birthday parties for girls turning 15 in Latino communities, and Pulido came out to more than 75 events. He made a show of singing his his hit “Desvelado” and also taking the time to engage with families and voters. Still, he was clear that he was focused on connecting with constituents, not performing for the masses. Trending Stories Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’ Adam Lambert on His Future With Queen, New Music, and Why the Show Must Go On As Nicki Minaj Rallied for Trump's GOP, Her Lawyers Told Court She Still Owes Them $295,000 Pink Floyd Marks ‘Animals’ 50th Anniversary With Reissue Featuring Unreleased Music

“I could foresee in the future maybe potentially doing a benefit and maybe using a concert here and there to help raise money for various causes that are really important to me,” he told Rolling Stone in October. “But public service to me is much more powerful. I do music for me because I enjoy it, but when you’re serving, it’s not for you. It’s for other people, and I think my life would be complete that way.”

When Pulido won his race this week, he celebrated by nodding to his music fans. “For more than 30 years, you’ve played my music at your weddings, your quinceañeras and your backyard barbecues,” he said.

These Panera Snowman Cups Are Only Available for a Day—Here’s How to Get One

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Panera Snowman Cup is giving the Starbucks Bearista Cup some cozy competition. Here's what you need to know about the limited-edition mystery boxes.

I don’t generally need an incentive to stop at Panera in December. Any of those delicious soups in a bread bowl is a fairly convincing argument on its own. But this year, the chain is giving us another reason to visit: collectible glass snowman cups, with a chance to get one free.

If you’ve been admiring the Starbucks Bearista cup, these little snowmen might also deserve a spot on your wish list. Panera’s version comes in a mystery box containing one of two designs, naughty or nice. The free cup offer runs on Dec. 3, and with only 12 available at each participating cafe, there are a few details to keep in mind before you go.

The limited-edition glass cups come in two designs inspired by the naughty-and-nice theme of Panera’s holiday packaging. Each mystery box holds one snowman cup, so you won’t know which you’ve received until you open it.

There are also a few new things to drink while you admire your cute cup. Panera’s new holiday menu, rolling out this week, includes peppermint white mocha and pistachio white mocha beverages. Both flavors are available as a latte, iced coffee or Frozen Java, finished with peppermint bark pieces or pistachio pieces. The iced coffees have sweet cream cold foam, and the lattes and Frozen Java drinks get whipped cream.

On Dec. 3, the first 12 customers at each participating U.S. cafe who make a qualifying purchase of $25 or more will receive a free Snowman Cup, while supplies last. There’s a limit of one per person, so you may want to make that Panera outing a breakfast date.

You’ll need to order inside the cafe at the cashier. Kiosk, Rapid Pick-Up, drive-thru, catering and delivery orders—including those placed through third-party sites—don’t qualify.

If you don’t get your hands on one of those first 12 boxes, you’ll have another way to find a snowman. A limited number of cups will also be available through the Panera Shop for $22 each. An online sale date hasn’t been announced yet, so keep an eye on the shop if you’re hoping to grab one.

Are you going to be first in line at Panera on Dec. 3? Let us know in the comments below!

Threat actor targets Indian fintechs on weekends when defenders aren’t watching

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

CERT-IN warns of ‘significant escalation’ in AI-powered attacks, some after victims tried to improve defences

India’s Computer Emergency Response Team has warned the nation’s financial services industry of a “significant escalation” in attacks by what it describes as an “AI-assisted financial threat actor” that has re-targeted victims after they flubbed remediation efforts.

CERT-IN first detected the attacks in October and issued advisory in which it warned digital lending platforms, fintech entities, payment service providers, and wallet operators that attackers were targeting internet-facing applications, exposed APIs and privileged endpoints. If the attackers found a way in, they tried to use payment APIs to steal money.

On Thursday, the CERT published an update in which it said it continues to hear of successful attacks, including from outfits that tried to remediate their systems after earlier incidents.

“The same entities have been deliberately re-targeted, including after incomplete remediation and attacks have been timed to coincide with holidays, weekends and other low-monitoring windows to delay detection and response,” the CERT warned.

CERT-IN has decided the attackers need a name and chose “CRAFTY FALCON.”

The security org has also revealed that the crew uses AI-enhanced tools including the ARTEX pen-testing tool, the Cairn attack search engine, and the CyberStrikeAI framework – all of which have been linked to users who appear to speak Chinese.

CRAFTY FALCON’s tactics see it go looking for internet-exposed environments used for testing or development, where it finds artefacts such as shared credentials, tokens, or network paths that help it move into production systems.

If it gets in, the crew re-writes victims’ applications so they do things like make unauthorized API calls. Victims’ defenses assume that only legitimate apps would call those APIs, so activity conducted by the re-written apps aren’t flagged as an attack.

Even if victims suspect an attack, they have a lot to cope with because CERT-IN says CRAFTY FALCON creates many “functionally identical variants refined in rapid succession; a pattern consistent with AI-generated code and iterative AI-assisted development.”

The crew also quickly exploits newly discovered vulnerabilities to keep its attacks fresh.

Once it gets a toehold, the criminal crew makes tiny payments of about $0.01 to test if its attacks work. If those transfers work, CRAFTY FALCON makes larger payments. When the target is a cryptocurrency exchange or virtual digital asset service provider, the crew steals wallet keys and empties cryptocurrency wallets.

Vendors of observability tools will be warming up their Indian sales teams today, because CERT-IN’s advice to Indian businesses includes improving their ability to log events, monitor for anomalies in real time, and send alerts. It also urged keeping test environments off the internet, and restricting access to cloud resources because CRAFTY FALCON is fond of using them to run its attacks.

CERT-IN also warns that the attacks are “active and escalating” and therefore worthy of 24x7 monitoring.

Interestingly, the CERT’s advisory mentions attacks on “entities” across banking, financial services, and insurance, but only names the kind of businesses mentioned above as having reported attacks.

The Register points this out because India is home to literally thousands of banks, many of them small operations that sometimes lack basic infosec protections. CRAFTY FALCON therefore has a target-rich environment in India should it choose to expand its activities. ®

Riley Green is just trying to find time to see his dog.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Riley Green is at the top of his game, with seven number one country songs, a spot as a coach on the long-running singing competition The Voice, currently in Season 30 on the 2026 TV schedule, and a well-received guest run on the Yellowstone spin-off Marshals. And while he might have recently made People Magazine’s Sexiest Man Alive issue, understandably so with his down-home appeal, he’s not looking to use that title to get himself out of bachelorhood anytime soon.

Whether he’s on tour as he takes his country crooning around the world or he’s in Los Angeles coaching a team of singers hoping to someday achieve the status of The Voice’s superstar coaches, Riley Green is booked and busy, and more concerned about finding time to spend with family than a girlfriend. While chatting with People about being selected for the sexiest list, he said he’s open to love but admitted to not even seeing his own dog in a month, so now is just probably not the best time. He explained:

I haven’t seen Carl, my dog, in a month. I struggle to think there’s a girl that, you know… I can barely make this lifestyle work. There’s so much growth in my career the last few years that that’s eventually going to stop. I mean, there is a level that I’ll get to and I’ll be completely content with it because I’ve already accomplished more than I ever thought I would... But I think it’s got to get to that place before I can really focus on me and what I want to do in my life.

It seems like the 38 year-old who likes to smack talk his fellow coaches on The Voice (especially O.G. American Idol winner Kelly Clarkson), has given the idea of love some definite thought. He’s very clear about the demands of his career and is riding that wave because he knows that his schedule likely won’t always be this hectic and the current high might not always be at the same level it is now.

For now, the “Worst Way” singer who even has his own customized Big Red Chair on The Voice is focusing on family. When you’re on the road 200-plus days a year, and also spending time in Los Angeles with coaching duties, hoping a member from Team Riley will take it all in Season 30, working in a FaceTime with your adorable nephew is about all the time you can spare. Green explained:

My lifestyle has just got to change a little bit. And I think that what gets me by now, without having those things that I’m kind of hoping for is, I get to FaceTime my nephew Joe, you know, and see what he’s doing and watch him play sports and catching up with my family. We’re all still really close, so they make it bearable in a pretty hectic lifestyle right now.

Constantly being on the road while touring can make any singer feel adrift, no matter how successful they are, so it’s great that Riley Green has close family that he can feel anchored by. Sure, the multi-talented performer who has added acting to his list of skills steals hearts and wins over the love of his growing fan base. But it’s probably a good thing that he wants to wait until he has the time to devote to focusing on just one woman before jumping into a relationship that he can’t fully be a part of. Autonomous combat drone maker Shield AI buys radar startup Echodyne for $5.3B

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Defense technology startup Shield AI Inc. said today it has agreed to buy the Bill Gates-backed radar startup Echodyne Inc. in a deal valued at $5.3 billion.

The acquisition is intended to enhance Shield’s autonomous drone warfighting capabilities, it said.

Echodyne is a 13-year-old startup that’s best known for its Metamaterials Electronically Scanned Array or MESA technology, which is basically just a fancy name for its next-generation solid-state radar systems that can be integrated with a wide range of weapons and monitoring systems. The technology is designed to detect much smaller targets than traditional radar, including the kinds of compact autonomous drones that have terrorized soldiers on the battlefields of Ukraine and in other modern conflicts.

The deal comes just a couple of months after Echodyne landed a $250 million contract with the U.S. Defense Department’s counter-drone task force JIATF-401, which will see it provide drone tracking technology to the U.S. military.

Echodyne’s advanced radar systems look like a natural fit for Shield AI, which is the developer of the X-Bat and V-Bat vertical takeoff and landing systems, known as VTOL drones. Shield’s V-Bat hit the headlines in the last couple of years after assisting the U.S. Coast Guard in tracking down drug traffickers looking to bring illicit cargoes into the U.S. via speedboats, and notably participated in a number of large maritime drug busts. Shield AI is also the developer of Hivemind, an autonomous software system for piloting unmanned drones, as well as pilotless boats and spacecraft.

Shield AI Chief Executive Gary Steele said the deal will enable his company to accelerate its goal of branching into three different areas of unmanned systems. “We are building a new-generation defense technology company consisting of three fast-growing, high-margin, mutually supporting business units: autonomy software, autonomous systems and mission systems,” he said.

Echodyne co-founder and CEO Eben Frankenberg said Shield AI is a natural partner for his company because it shares the view that autonomous systems must be able to sense their environment to achieve their desired mission outcomes. “By joining Shield, we’re creating a company with the size and breadth to deliver even more value to our broad range of defense, government and commercial customers around the globe,” he insisted.

According to Steele, Echodyne will continue to operate as a standalone entity, as a separate business unit within Shield AI’s mission systems business. Frankenberg will remain with the company, where he will report to Steele as part of its senior executive team.

Echodyne is not going to layoff any staff, and nobody will be asked to relocate either, he said. In the longer term, the companies expect tighter integration of Echodyne’s radar systems with Shield AI’s drones.

The deal will see $4.8 billion paid out upon closing, with the other $500 million dependent on Echodyne meeting certain performance targets, which have not been disclosed. Shield AI said it was able to fund the deal with a combination of its own cash and a $2.5 billion cash injection that came via a Series H funding round led by Blackstone and JPMorgan Chase, which was also announced today.

Shield AI has a history of making decisive acquisitions. Its V-Bat and X-Bat drones are heavily based on technology developed by a company called Martin UAV Systems Inc. that it acquired in 2021. In June, it purchased Aechelon Technologies Inc., a developer of high-fidelity simulation and physics-based sensor modeling software, for an undisclosed price.

Steele added that the company may not be done with acquisitions, and will continue to look at making more deals in order to accelerate its growth strategy.

New Hampshire

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The penultimate state! Wow. Not only did it feel amazing to leave Vermont (finally!) but walking into such a highly anticipated state was surreal. Also Singe’s home state and homecoming! Even though I had never hiked in a lot of the prior states, I had at least visited a majority of them. But from the moment I stepped foot into New Hampshire, everything was brand new to me. I was trying to convey that New Hampshire and Maine would be new states for me but what I ended up saying was, “New state forever. Or…wait. How would a normal person say that?” To which Loon said, “How about ‘I’ve never been here before.'” lol.

Singe, Loon, and I walked across the Connecticut River into New Hampshire on a sweltering summer day in early August. Our first order of business? Jump right into the river. Before strolling into Hanover, right after the bridge, you can take a left, walk to the Dartmouth dock, ignore all the signs about it being for students and staff only, and jump right in. It felt AMAZING. I cannot emphasize enough how hot the sun was and how exposed the concrete bridge is. The river was full of weird cold pockets that we enjoyed to the max. Once we had cooled down, we finally walked into Hanover! I was super excited because I’m a little nerd who loves college campuses and Hanover being both a college town and a hiker town had always stuck with me.

Hanover was so cute and Dartmouth was so beautiful. Shocking. We found the Dartmouth Outdoors Club and left our packs in their care. Then we walked around Hanover and cashed in on some awesome hike friendly deals! We got free pizza for hikers and were completely entranced by the Housecleaning Games on ESPN 8: The Ocho. My favorite part? A poster that said “Ouchtown: Population YOU.” Incredible. We got handmade ice cream at Red Kite Candy which remains one of my most memorable ice cream stops on trail. I had Strawberry Balsamic. Thank you for asking. We grabbed our packs and walked around campus a little before getting picked up to stay with one of Singe’s good friends in town. It was so exciting to get a look into Singe’s life and meet the characters and friends she’s been talking about all trail!

We had an awesome night getting to know one of Singe’s oldest friends and having a homemade pot roast. YUM. Also this night, I got an email that I got off the waitlist for a marathon the first weekend of October. In Connecticut I started toying with the idea of running a marathon after trail just to see what would happen. When I received this email offering me a spot, I went for it. Turns out, this is the same marathon that Loon’s friend, who we have seen multiple times on trail, was training for! Done and done. The rest of the hike, this marathon was a sort of bit. I would forget I signed up and someone would mention it and I’d be like “oh RIGHT! The marathon! Yes, of course.” What a bit.

The next day, we were able to leave most of our things at Singe’s friend’s house because we were staying with another Singe friend connection the next night! We walked over Velvet Rocks, right off the campus of Dartmouth, and to our pick up spot near the Ice Cream Man’s house where I discovered my new favorite Key Lime popsicle. We got to hang out with the cutest dog’s at Singe’s friend’s parents’ house and enjoy their company and a delicious home-cooked meal.

We were back to it with full packs this time. We were coming up close to Singe’s house and sections of trail she’s done several times. The plan was for Loon and I to spend the night out on trail but Singe was going to get picked up by her partner and stay at her house. We would join them the next day. It feels weird to be apart from Singe for even a night but I loved having a night with just Loon. We plotted to stay at Hexacuba Shelter because it seems weird and cool. Like I’ve said many times before, you have to look for reasons to get excited for the day. Hexacuba is a shelter in a hexagon shape with a pentagon shaped privy. Obviously, we had to see that. It turned out to be just the two of us at the shelter that night so we threw up our tents inside to have some protection from the bugs. We had the sweetest night camping and laughing together.

We climbed up and over Smarts Mountain and onto the fire tower the day before Hexacuba and Cube Mountain the morning after Hexacuba. I loved both! On top of Cube Mountain, we found a rude comment someone had left on a post of mine that had been shared and honestly had a field day with it. This person’s mean comment (and others they would continue to leave on my posts later) became one of our tramily’s favorite inside jokes. We took it well and not seriously but a little reminder: If you don’t have anything nice to say, don’t say it! This can really hurt people! Especially online!

Anywho! After clearing these two mountains, covering more ground, and taking a perfect midday lake swim, Singe picked us up and we went back to her house! It was so fun to see where our trail bestie lived after talking about it for so many miles. Spoiler: It’s beautiful. We took a zero with her and got to see some of New Hampshire that’s off trail too. I didn’t know I was going to fall in love with New Hampshire so quickly! There was a lot of anticipation because as soon as we left the comfiness of Singe’s house, we would be entering the Whites. But of course we were able to scheme a slack-pack and were able to not bring full packs over Mt Moosilauke by dropping our things off at The Notch Hostel a day early.

I was quite nervous and intimidated of the Whites before we started. But step by step, we ascended the mountain. I actually flew up to the top pretty fast. It’s difficult, but I think I had it built up so much in my mind that it was better than I expected. I did fall in a little tiny creek on the way up (lol) but it didn’t get me down. And when I broke the treeline? I was so speechless and energized that nothing really mattered. I kept squealing to myself out of excitement. The terrain is gorgeous and we had a beautiful day. I loved being able to see the path ahead of me and the progress I was making pushing to the summit. There were a ton of people hiking at the top but I was in my own little world taking it all in…until I heard a group to my left singing out “You’re a bear and I’m a girlllll.” AKA the Song of the Summer AKA the song I sang to a bear when I had a close encounter back in New York. I looked over and our trail cousins, The Binguses were sitting together serenading me. What a welcome to the top! This was the first of several mountain top reunions that kept happening in the Whites. So much fun. I went over and had lunch with them and waited for Singe and Loon to summit. We hadn’t seen the Binguses since we were all in New York City together in early July! We also got to meet a new Bingus they’d picked up along the way in Vermont.

We all hung out together at the top. Apparently some kind of eclipse was happening but not a full eclipse so we didn’t end up sticking around to see it from the top since the weather looked like it might be turning too. We all bunched up together for a picture on the summit that a day hiker took. It looked like she was taking several. She said to us, “How many do you want?” So we figured we’d have good options. But when she returned the phone, we had exactly three pictures. lol. Us Golden Girls and the Binguses all tackled the steep and endless descent together. We stopped a few times to look at the pretty waterfalls that follow the trail. It was so fun to be back with them again for this hike. We all got to the parking lot and waited for the shuttle to the Notch. A few of the Binguses weren’t staying at the hostel so they found a hitch into town.

We got picked up to head to the Notch by the owner, Serena, who also happens to be Singe’s good friend and climbing partner in normal life! She was awesome! Please stay at the Notch! They are so great and have their systems figured out so smoothly. Serena told the 6 of us in the shuttle (3 Golden Girls + 3 of the Binguses) that a local outdoor apparel company, Burgeon, wanted to invite 6 hikers for a pan pasta dinner tonight. So of course we were like, “we’ll we’re 6 hikers…” And voila! We checked in, showered, and an hour later we were on our way to have one of the best meals of my life. Three workers at Burgeon ate with us in their on-site stock room. They were cool and fun and we had a great time. This was one of the coolest and most exciting trail magics we had. Loon asked if they needed a Colorist to work for them since I work in fashion as a colorist! Thanks for trying to get me a job, Loon!

After the dinner, Singe’s partner offered to pick us up and take us to Super Secret Ice Cream. An ice cream shop in Bethlehem, NH that Singe had been talking up since probably day 2 of me knowing her. As an ice cream connoisseur and an AT Dairy-Blazer, I couldn’t wait. We left the boy Binguses behind and Loon, Singe, Outlaw, and I got ice cream. I am absolutely obsessed with this place. The branding, the colors, the unique small-batch flavors, the ice cream flights, the posters–all of it is perfect. New favorite ice cream. I think it’s extremely tragic that I live so far away from it and also probably for the best. If you’re even remotely close though, take the detour! Best ice cream I’ve had on trail and also in years hands down.

All of this together made for such a magical day and one of the standouts from trail. Tackling a hard and anticipated mountain but feeling strong? That view?! Reuniting with our friends unexpectedly! Surprise trail magic pasta dinner?! AND SUPER SECRET ICE CREAM?! What a way to kick off the Whites.

The next day we slackpacked the Kinsmens with The Notch. They have a deal that if you stay two nights, the slackpack is included. Yes please! The Kinsmens were some of the most difficult parts of the Whites. It wasn’t my worst day there, but quite a challenge. The final ascent up is basically bouldering/rock scrambling. I was so glad to not have my full pack weight, but I did feel extremely rushed trying to catch the shuttle back to the Notch. The options were a 4pm or 7pm. We got a tiny bit of a later start that ate into our hiking time. Thus, I ended my hike around 4:10pm while feeling extremely rushed and stressed the last part of the day. The trail was super rocky and frustrating. We missed the shuttle but since Singe did know the staff well, they came and picked us up. Thank you! When we got back to the hostel, someone had decided to make dinner for everyone as trail magic. What a godsend. Two covered dinners in a row. I was so incredibly exhausted after the Kinsmens that I probably would have cried if I had to figure out my own dinner. Moosilauke felt like a warm up but the Kinsmens felt like an ass-kicking. I was worried more of that was to come but Singe told me she thinks the Kinsmens are some of the hardest 4kers in the Whites and it shouldn’t feel worse than that which really helped. Tomorrow, we’d be back to full packs and really getting into it!

There is a challenge in New Hampshire hiking called the New Hampshire 48. This refers to a group of 48 prominent peaks all above 4k. The AT goes right over a few of them, and grazes along the side of a few others. Some people choose to tag the summits and take the detours to hit as many summits as they can. In hiking this pursuit of summits is called “Peak Bagging.” While we went through the Whites, Loon was all in on peak bagging as many of the 48 as she could. She was a rockstar! Singe and I supported her and encouraged her but did not have the same drive. I was too worried about my ankles on all the rocky ups and downs that I didn’t want to risk anything by doing extras. Because of this, Singe and I sometimes had slightly different days than Loon. Like for example, when Loon walked an extra like 7 miles to get 4 extra peaks in a day and Singe and I walked 5 extra miles the next day to be able to go get ice cream. It’s important to know where your priorities are.

Still, on the AT, I got my fair share of the 48 done. The trail follows a section of the Whites called the Pemigewasset Loop (or Pemi Loop) for about 10 miles after the Kinsmens. Most notably, it follows along Franconia Ridge which is an exposed ridge walk that goes over multiple peaks. You can see the circle of the Pemi Loop through the mountains while up on the ridge. I absolutely loved Franconia Ridge. It’s like nothing else on the trail. It most closely reminded my of a hike I had done in the Dolomites! And yet it’s here! Just sitting up in New Hampshire! Wow! Franconia is known for being beautiful but also extreme due to the exposure. Like everything in the Whites, it’s quite steep (like straight up rock ladders for miles) to get up to the top and then open to strong winds and weather with nowhere to hide. We were lucky to have gorgeous weather throughout the Whites. While we did have some wind (including being pushed over by the wind a few times), it was nothing unmanageable or unpleasant on Franconia. Still, the exposure is tough. There was even a Search and Rescue on this section the same day we were up there. But we got through it just fine along with my new can of Honey Mustard Pringles that we became obsessed with and didn’t last more than 6 miles. We were in such awe the whole time. When I first got to the top of the ridge, I ran into McMurphy! Another mountain top reunion! Loon started a little further back to get more peaks, so we also reunited with her on a summit!

Towards the end of the ridge, the novelty had worn off a little and we were ready to get out of the wind and set up camp for the night. We went down and then up and over Mount Garfield where I had another summit reunion! I ran into Sniper, a woman I had met in Georgia and North Carolina. She hiked the AT years ago and is attempting a Yo-yo of the full trail this year! This means she hiked from Georgia to Maine and will turn around and hike all the way back down to Georgia. Incredible!! It was so cool to run into her and see her still crushing it! After leaving her and wishing her the best on the rest of the journey, I made my way down to the Garfield Campsite where of course we ran into several other friends!

The Whites are known for their Hut-to-Hut system where you can stay in little lodges along the way. This is awesome for a stand alone trip, but not really an option for thru-hikers. They are quite expensive (especially multiple nights in a row) and require reservations far in advance. That would be nearly impossibly to schedule on a thru-hiker’s flexible and ever-changing timeline. Stealth camping is also not allowed/highly discouraged in the Whites due to the extreme weather and the delicate nature of the alpine plants. There are designated campgrounds throughout the Whites that each have caretakers. They are all first come first serve, but the caretakers are great at finding spots for everyone so we were never too stressed about it. At this first campsite, we registered for a thru-hiker pass with the caretaker that allows us to camp at all the campgrounds for a discounted price and also get free pastries and soup when we stop into the huts! Wahoo! We opted to stay in the shelter the first night because of the wind.

The next day we started off our hike by basically having to walk straight down a sort of rocky waterfall. The Whites are on another level. We made it to the Galehead Hut for lunch where we cashed in our free soup pass. Then we continued up South Twin Mountain. For some reason, when we summited, I could not get “Breathe” by Faith Hill out of my head and I continued to sing it as my anthem the rest of trail. South Twin was a long push up, but the descent felt flat compared to the climb. What a treat! We went to a campsite a little off the path since there weren’t too many options otherwise. You’d either have to do a really short day or a really long one. Given the strenuous terrain, we opted for a short day. Singe and I got to the campsite around 3pm while Loon went off to go bag three more peaks! It felt really nice to have time to relax and recover. I don’t think I got to camp before 4pm since North Carolina. We stayed in the shelter this night too and watched a beautiful sunrise from the comfort of our sleeping bags the next morning.

Our next day in the Whites was a bit of a Choose Your Own Adventure. This was one of the only nights on trail (the only night on trail?) that Singe, Loon, and I were all apart. I had a field day telling people we were all apart because we were fighting. Nobody believed it for a second. We had a bit of an easier day planned passing by Zealand Hut and aiming for Ethan Pond Shelter. But Singe’s friend told us she could meet us at the trailhead for Zealand Hut and take us to lunch in Bethlehem and then return us to trail. Loon decided to pass and stick with the original plan since she was already doing so many extra miles and peaks but all Singe had to tell me was we could get Super Secret Ice Cream again and I was in. Singe left a little before me in the morning but we met up at Zealand Hut. It was super special to be there with her because it was the first place she decided she ever wanted to Thru-Hike back in college. <3 We took a side trail that was super flat about 2.5 miles to a parking lot. I timed myself on how long it took from Zealand to the lot because I had the idea that I might try to return later for Work-For-Stay if the timing worked out. We had a great lunch and ice cream of course and were returned to the trailhead. It felt like we snuck off campus in the middle of the day with our cool older sister. We were cutting it close but I wanted to try for the work for stay at Zealand since it’s a unique thru-hiker experience and I didn’t know when else the timing would work out to try. I ZOOMED through the 2.5 miles back up to Zealand in record time and ta-da! I was granted one of the three work for stay spots that night! Singe ended up camping solo sort of in between Loon at Ethan Pond and myself at the hut.

Two more hikers I knew rolled in and we hung around the pretty waterfall waiting for our duties to begin. We got leftovers from the dinner that night and then were assigned the tasks of sweeping, wiping down tables, and reorganizing the freezer for the hut croo. In exchange, we got the dinner leftovers and were able to sleep in the dining room which was much appreciated since it rained that night. We had to get up super early so the staff could make breakfast for the paying guests. We hung around outside until breakfast leftovers were set out and then we descended on those. From there we made our way to the AMC Highland Center where Loon, Singe, and I met back up for a zero. I had a job interview over the phone the next day and I was touched by Singe and Loon’s efforts to make sure we were somewhere I could take the call with reliable service. We were excited for a rest too since the Whites are so demanding even when dropping the number of miles you’re doing each day. The Highland Center was also super neat because we got to run into our friend, Mosey, who we met during the first half of trail and was an original Bingus! The Binguses also trickled in over the next day and we got to catch up with them as well!

The interview went well (though did not ultimately land me a job…but that’s story for Maine) and I was feeling excited. There was no laundry so we did our best to freshen our clothes up in the bunkhouse sink. We enjoyed taking over the on-site library to do our nails and watch TV on our phones together. It was nice to have a little break before the Presidential Range or “The Prezies.” We actually lucked out by taking a zero for my interview because it let some bad weather pass and gave us a perfect weather window later!

After our time at the Highland Center and mapping out our next stretch, we made our way back up into the range. We made our way to the Mizpah Hut and Nauman Campground. Mizpah was one of my favorite huts. There was something more remote, cozy, and mellow about it more than the others I had seen. The Croo was also very friendly. We checked on a work-for-stay just to see but they didn’t have any so we camped at the Nauman Campground next door. Singe, Loon, and I got an entire tenting platform to ourselves! We didn’t know it then, but this would be the last night the three of us would camp together without anyone else. We had a magical time. Somehow, it wasn’t too cold so we decided to cowboy camp on our platform under the clear sky. We watched a beautiful sunset and had such a fun and special night. We were laughing so much. This night was such a highlight on trail. We slept under the stars and I remember waking up at different times during the night and seeing how the milky way was moving across the sky. What an experience.

The next day we got breakfast leftovers at Mizpah Hut with another female hiker who was traveling with her partner. I think she enjoyed having some girl time with us and we loved it too! We made our way from the hut up to the Mount Pierce summit (President Pierce not Pierce Brosnan apparently) and were officially in the Prezies! The first summit was not so bad but we were hyped! We could see the others stretching before us, most of which we’d tackle that day! We took a moment at the summit to talk about how excited we were. Before we continued on, Singe said, “Does anyone have words they’d like to share about Pierce before we continue?” which made me laugh all day thinking about it.

Mount Eisenhower was next and it was so beautiful! We had a gorgeous day and could see Mount Washington’s summit for the first time! Though it was quite windy, Eisenhower was one of my favorite mountains in the Prezies because the 360 views are so stunning! What a view of Washington! After a snack break and some photos, we kept it moving before getting too cold. We went up and over Little Monroe and Mount Monroe where we got a look at Lakes of the Clouds Hut where we would be staying. We decided to go ahead and book a full guest spot for Lakes of the Clouds because we wanted to experience the hut culture as guests and because it’s tricky to find a place to camp if you show up to this hut and there’s no room for thru hikers. Everything is exposed and there’s no camping around. By this point, you’re at the base of Mount Washington and frankly, out of options. We felt like we had such a magical day hopping from summit to summit and squealing about the views and the weather. Also, mileage wise, Lakes of the Clouds is not so far from Nauman Campground so we made it to the hut in really good time. Such good time, in fact, that we decided to go ahead and summit Mount Washington that afternoon! Originally we were going to wait until the next day but when you get good weather and you have the time…don’t waste that opportunity. We decided to go up the 1.5 trail to the summit with slackpacks and take a trail around the next day.

Washington was….fine. Loon, Singe, and I pressed play on the Hamilton soundtrack at the same time to get us up the mountain. It’s so strange to make your way up this infamous beast of a mountain only to be met with a bunch of ill prepared tourists who drove or took the Cog Railroad up. It was crowded and touristy, but hey, at least we weren’t hit with 100mph winds! We didn’t stay terribly long before making our way back to the hut. We knew 3 of the Binguses were making their way to the hut tonight too and we were excited to see them. Our friend, Mosey, was also able to work on-site at Lakes of the Clouds that day and spend the night too! The Binguses had to come from further so they had more of an exhausting day but and were wiped. Still, it was great to see them and spend some time with them after dinner when everyone went outside to take in one of our best sunsets of trails. I heard Brewski say, “We got to do stars tonight” about remembering to stargaze and it cracked me up enough that that phrase became a part of our lexicon the rest of trail.

While the first half of the Prezies were a dream, the second half were a bit of a nightmare. I did not enjoy day two of the traverse. The landscape was completely exposed all day and so rocky and unstable. It felt endless and frustrating. Maybe everything we’d been doing physically caught up to me and the novelty wore off? Either way, yikes. We made our way around Washington towards Jefferson where I did decide to tag the summit even though it was technically off trail. I got to the top and felt very regretful that I wasted the effort. I did not go up to tag Mount Adams. We made our way to Madison Hut where we ran into half of the Binguses again and commiserated about the day with them. I remember us telling Brewski “Can you please fake a medical emergency so we can all get airlifted out? You’re still on your parents’ insurance so it has to be you.”

We were trying to explore other options or side trails for getting down. We were going to meet Singe’s parents this night and stay with them in an airbnb and zero the next day but the idea of having a bed to sleep in felt too far away while we were traversing around. We wanted very badly to not have to go up and over Mount Madison and down to Pinkham Notch but there was really no other route. Eventually, we just had to go. The ascent up Madison really wasn’t so awful but the trail down? If you can even call it a trail? Was the absolute worst part of the entire AT for me. Everything was so exposed and unstable and endless and I felt like I would be stuck on the mountain forever. The progress was glacial and the steps were slow. I was without a doubt angry hiking. I remember looking for any sliver of hope and saying, “It’s only .9 left to the treeline!” and Loon, the beautiful and literal hiker she is, said, “And then it gets even steeper!” To which I yelled something like, “SHUT UP WHY WOULD YOU SAY THAT TO ME?!” We weren’t really angry at each other and it’s a moment we laughed about a lot throughout the rest of trail. I seriously think the descent down Madison is extremely dangerous and I cannot believe it isn’t talked about more. ANYWAYS. We did get down to the treeline and for me and my knees, it felt at least a little better walking on dirt and having trees to cling to if it was too steep.

We made it down to the Osgood Campsite where a random man appeared out of nowhere and tried to give me unsolicited advice. I was not having it. We kept joking that throughout this hike our faith in humanity has gone up but our tolerance of men has gone down. Once Singe and Loon made it down too, we took the original AT instead of the detour so that we could make it to Pinkham Notch for Singe’s parents to pick us up. What looked flat on the map ended up being flat with some random sharp ups and downs. Normally this would be okay, but when we’re already feeling so worn out, this was maddening. One of Singe’s friends had originally planned to meet us SOBO during the hike but wasn’t able to make it all the way up so she left us a bag of trail magic which we found towards the end of the day. It was a really sweet mental boost. We were about .2 from the Mount Washington Auto Road and 2 miles from Pinkham Notch. It was almost 7pm and we knew we were losing daylight quickly. Loon ran ahead to see if we could hitch down to the auto road entrance with the last of the cars leaving Mount Washington. The road up closes at 6pm but we hoped we could catch a hitch from some stragglers on the way out. We saw exactly two cars. One was a minivan that slowed down to tell us they didn’t have room even though we could clearly see they did. I started to cry. The next was a truck. I continued to pathetically cry while we asked for a ride to hopefully sway the decision. Unfortunately, it was a Washington worker who wasn’t allowed to give a ride in the work vehicle. We completely understood and that was disappointing but fine with us. The real kick in the pants was actually when he said, “The quickest thing for you to do will be to take the AT 2 miles down to Pinkham Notch.” WE KNOW SIR! My knees and feet were beat to crap from the day and I knew I didn’t have two more trail miles in the dark in me. We decided to walk down the auto road to where Singe’s dad could pick us up.

Loon, Singe, and I stayed in an airbnb with Singe’s parents near North Conway. We had a zero where we got our errands done and were also able to do a big fat nothing. Thank you Mr. & Mrs. Singe for your generosity once again!! The Binguses also took a zero at Mosey’s so we were all aligned for the next stretch! On Sunday, we set back out for the stretch between The Wildcats (where we listened to High School Musical) and the Carters (where I listened to Beyonce). We stayed overnight at Imp Shelter. The weather was drizzly but still could have been worse and was better than we expected. This is quite a steep section to do in the wet. Singe and I got to the shelter first so we watched Hacks on her phone before others showed up. I did spill an entire new bag of mini M&Ms but luckily my spirits were restored enough to think this was funny and not too tragic.

The next day, us Golden Girls and half of the Binguses hiked from Imp Shelter over Mount Moriah and down to the 1900 mile marker! We are right on the heels of Maine! We were greeted by The Ice Cream Man who gave us Klondike bars from his camper. We met some cool SOBOs and took pictures at the final 1900s mile marker before getting picked up by a friend I had made hiking out in the Dolomites last year. We met on a practice hike I did in Italy (I used practicing for the AT as an excuse to hike in really cool places and I recommend you do the same). I actually twisted my ankle (shocking) and he helped wrap it up since he is a SAR volunteer in the Whites! We got to talking while staying at some of the same huts and he told me to reach out when I came through on the AT and he and his wife would host me and whomever! It felt surreal to know this other milestone had been met. I was getting to see the New Hampshire connection I had before hiking.

My friend and his wife were so lovely and generous to us. We showered and enjoyed their company. My friend told us stories of his best SARs. We made taco bowls for them in exchange for the homestay and Strider made an apple pie from scratch!! The next day, we stayed talking over breakfast way longer than we planned because they were just so fun. My friend ended up letting me drive his car back to trail and leave a pin for him to pick it up later. Like I said, they were extremely generous to us. It felt a little funny to drive my trail friends in his car. We made it to the trail head and continued on! We had a long day pushing to the first shelter in Maine! We went up and over Mt Success which is the last mountain in New Hampshire. At the top, we learned the news that Dolly Parton had died. RIP. We also found one of our favorite FarOut comments on trail. If you scroll to the very beginning of the Mt Success comments it says “take that Dad!” Nice.

Soon we crossed into Maine! It immediately became about 10x more extreme just like everyone had promised. But also 10x more breathtaking. We saw a double rainbow while crossing over the Goose Eye mountains which I like to think was for Dolly. I thought maybe I’d feel more emotional crossing into Maine. Certainly it’s a big moment, but there’s still a lot of work to be done. Still, I did it. At the very least, my legs and feet have taken me from Georgia to Maine.

Parrish McWhorter is taking on the AT! Originally from Georgia, Parrish has spent the last 5 years in Philadelphia performing stand up comedy, working in fashion, traveling, and dreaming about her first thru-hike attempt. She is most excited for the meeting new people and peeing outside and least excited for missing her dog, Felix Thompson. You can follow along on The Trek or on Instagram @theparrishm.

Stinks that someone posted rude comments on your previous posts! What’s wrong with people? I really enjoy your posts. You and Singe and Loon seem like you had the most fun hiking the AT of any of the bloggers I read! I know there were some really hard days, but you always found a way to laugh through the experience. 🙂 Thanks for continuing to document your trek now that you’re home!

FC Cincinnati vs Nashville SC - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

By Ben Sully | 06 Nov 2026 01:11, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 03:30

FC Cincinnati will look to secure a spot in the first round of the MLS playoffs when they take on Nashville SC at TQL Stadium on Saturday.

Nashville head into MLS Decision Day with their fate already sealed, having clinched their first-ever Supporters' Shield.

Cincinnati are sitting in seventh spot in the Eastern Conference after winning 12, drawing 11 and losing 10 of their 33 matches this season.

Pat Noonan's side have come into form at just the right time, having dropped just two points from their last four matches since suffering consecutive defeats against New England Revolution and Columbus Crew.

Cincinnati recorded home and away wins in back-to-back games against DC United, before they played out a thrilling 2-2 draw against Inter Miami at Nu Stadium.

The Orange and Blue then returned to winning ways with a narrow 1-0 home victory against Philadelphia Union, which came courtesy of Ayoub Jabbari's third goal of the season.

Having already guaranteed some form of post-season action, Cincinnati will secure a spot in the first round with a win against Nashville, but a failure to claim all three points will leave them open to being overtaken by Columbus and Orlando City, who currently occupy the two wild-card round positions.

The hosts can take some confidence from the fact they have won two of their last three MLS home games against Nashville, including a 2-1 success in September 2025 at TQL Stadium.

Nashville will already have one eye on the first round of the playoffs after clinching their first MLS Supporters' Shield with a 2-0 win over Montreal last month.

BJ Callaghan's side went on to play out a goalless draw against Philadelphia Union before coming from two goals down to pull off a 3-2 victory over New York Red Bulls last time out.

Sam Surridge netted a stoppage-time winner in that comeback success, which extended the club's impressive 17-game unbeaten home run in the MLS.

In fact, they have gone nine matches without defeat at any venue since losing away to Toronto FC on September 27, so they will be desperate to carry that undefeated streak into the playoffs as they continue to work towards their ultimate goal of winning the MLS Cup for the first time in their history.

Having tied the MLS single-season points record, Nashville will take the outright record if they are able to add at least a point to their 74-point tally in their final outing of the regular season.

Nashville will certainly fancy their chances of claiming a positive result after running out 4-0 winners in their most recent meeting with Cincinnati at Geodis Park in August.

Cincinnati defender Tyrell Malacia is unlikely to prove his fitness in time, having been forced to sit out the last three matches with an ankle injury.

The hosts are also without the services of Julian Malatini and Miles Robinson, while Teenage Hadebe is hoping to prove his fitness after being withdrawn with an apparent injury issue in the win over Philadelphia.

Evander is in contention to start the final regular season game after featuring as a half-time substitute last weekend.

As for the visitors, they have reported no fresh injury concerns ahead of Saturday's away trip, which should give Callaghan a full squad to choose from.

The likes of Daniel Lovitz, Edvard Tagseth, Alex Muyl and Warren Madrigal could come into Callaghan's thinking if he wants to manage minutes ahead of the playoffs.

That said, Surridge will surely be keen to retain his place as he looks to add to the 19 goals he has scored in the regular season.

Celentano; Smith, Hagglund, Hadebe; Mboma, Bucha, Nwobodo, Evander, Ramirez; Barlow, Denkey

Schwake; Najar, Palacios, Woledzi, Lovitz; Espinoza, Yazbek, Tagseth, Muyl; Madrigal, Surridge

Nashville carry a nine-game unbeaten run into Saturday's fixture, and while there may be changes to their starting lineup, we think they will do enough to extend their undefeated streak with a draw.

Austin FC vs Portland Timbers - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

By Nsidibe Akpan | 06 Nov 2026 01:09

Austin FC host Portland Timbers in a Major League Soccer Decision Day fixture at Q2 Stadium on Saturday.

A win would clinch a playoff place for Portland, while Austin, already eliminated, play the last match of Davy Arnaud’s interim spell before Jim Curtin takes over.

Austin FC sit 14th in the Western Conference and were eliminated from playoff contention by consecutive defeats to Real Salt Lake on October 28 and LA Galaxy on October 31.

Arnaud has been in charge on an interim basis since Nico Estevez was dismissed on May 18, and Curtin, the former Philadelphia Union head coach, takes over on December 1 or at the end of the season, whichever is later.

The Galaxy defeat was settled early, with Joao Klauss scoring in the 10th minute, Hirving Lozano in the 38th and Robert Taylor in the 48th to leave Austin three goals down before the hour.

Ervin Torres pulled one back in the 54th minute with a long-range strike for his second career MLS goal, set up by Micah Burton, who was making his first MLS start, and Jorge Alastuey, before Sergi Roberto made it 4-1 in the 66th minute.

Damian Las made two saves in the 35th minute to keep the score at 2-0 at that stage, and Riley Thomas came off the bench to make his MLS debut.

Austin have scored in each of their last seven matches, having last been held scoreless by FC Dallas on September 20.

Myrto Uzuni and Christian Ramirez both have nine goals and would reach double figures with a goal on Saturday.

Austin also won the last meeting 2-1 at Providence Park on August 29, with Facundo Torres scoring the opener and Oleksandr Svatok netting the winner in stoppage time.

Portland Timbers are eighth in the West on 43 points, with 12 wins, seven draws and 14 defeats from their 33 matches, and a run of three straight victories has lifted them into a wild-card position.

Marti Cifuentes’s side were in a seven-game winless run between early September and mid-October, so a third successive playoff appearance in his first season would mark a sharp turnaround.

The three wins came against Charlotte FC, St. Louis City and Houston Dynamo, all of whom are heading to the postseason, and the Timbers scored nine goals across them.

The 3-1 victory over Houston on November 1 featured a 18-8 shot advantage, with six efforts on target compared with one for the visitors.

Vincent Janssen, a midseason signing, scored his fifth goal of the season against Houston and added two assists, the first of his MLS career.

Kristoffer Velde scored his 11th goal of the season in the same game, and American Soccer Analysis rated him the most valuable outfield player in MLS outside Lionel Messi since the World Cup break.

David Da Costa scored the opener and took his team-leading assist tally to 11 from the corner that Janssen headed in for the third.

The Timbers rank ninth in MLS for goals scored but 22nd for goals conceded, and they have kept only two clean sheets this season.

A win clinches a playoff place and keeps seventh place, and a round-one berth, within reach, while a draw only secures qualification with help from other results.

Portland Timbers Major League Soccer form:

Austin will do without Chris Applewhite (quad) and Jon Bell (lower abdomen) for Matchday 34, Zan Kolmanic is suspended following his red card against LA Galaxy.

Uzuni and Ramirez are both available to chase double figures, and Facundo Torres, scorer of the August opener in Portland is also available.

Portland have six players listed as out: Alex Bonetig (shoulder), Brandon Bye (knee), Jimer Fory (concussion), Zac McGraw (back), Juan Mosquera (foot/ankle) and goalkeeper Trey Muse (concussion).

Janssen, Velde, Da Costa and Ariel Lassiter are all available again.

Las; Desler, Sanchez, Biro; Burton, Sabovic, E. Torres, Alastuey; Placheta, Gallagher, Ramirez

Portland Timbers possible starting lineup:

Pantemis; Hernandez, Surman,  Waterman, Smith; Izoita,  Caicedo, Da Costa; Lassiter, Velde, Janssen

Portland arrive with the clearer motivation and the stronger recent evidence, as nine goals in three wins against playoff-bound opponents sit alongside a front line in which Janssen, Velde and Da Costa are all producing.

Austin conceded three goals inside 48 minutes at the Galaxy, are heading for a bottom-two finish in the West, and have nothing to play for beyond individual milestones and the chance to give their academy players further minutes.

The Timbers have not lost in Austin since 2021, and their need for a result should decide a game in which both sides score.
