Olivia Rodrigo, Noted ‘Sex and the City’ Fan, Recruits Cynthia Nixon For Christmas Video

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Olivia Rodrigo has made her Sex and the City dreams come true. The pop superstar has recruited Cynthia Nixon for the “Take Me Home (4 Christmas)” video, her first-ever holiday song.

The video features Rodrigo, Nixon, and SNL cast member Ben Marshall (who is also part of the comedy trio Please Don’t Destroy). It starts with Rodrigo cleaning up after an all-night rager, stepping over Marshall as he lies passed on the floor. She envisions him bringing her home to his family during the holidays and plays out some scenarios that are very The Family Stone-inspired. Nixon takes on the role of her welcoming future-in-law as Rodrigo bonds with the family, joins in on charades, makes gingerbread houses, and helps carve out a Christmas bird.

In the days leading up to the video, Rodrigo posted a clip of the crew playing a game of Celebrity, only everyone had “Olivia Rodrigo” written on their headbands. She also joined Nixon by the Christmas tree in another video, which played the Sex and the City theme.

Earlier this year, Rodrigo told Jimmy Fallon that Sex and the City is her favorite show, and that she’s “watched every single episode maybe three times.” She said the series inspired her recent album You Seem Pretty Sad for a Girl So in Love, particularly the relationship between Nixon’s character Miranda Hobbs and Steve Brady (David Eigenberg).

“When Miranda and Steve are getting back together she’s crying and she’s like, ‘Steve, any time something funny I just want to tell you,'” she told Fallon. “And I remember watching that and being like, ‘Oh my God, I have to write a song about this.’” She did exactly that, inserting the line in “Maggots for Brains.” (“Everything feels moldy like the fruit that’s in my fridge/And everything that’s funny, I wish I could tell to him.”) Following her Fallon interview, Nixon posted a video of her dancing to “Drivers License” in her kitchen. Google, TikTok, and Meta are suing California to stop a law that bans “psychologically exploitative features” for kids under 16

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Google, TikTok, and Meta have each filed suit against California in an attempt to block enforcement of a law that protects kids under 16 from “psychologically exploitative features” on social media platforms.

Governor Gavin Newsom signed the law, Assembly Bill 1709, into law this past September. “We want our children–and every California ​kid–to grow up in a world where technology supports their wellbeing, rather than exploits their vulnerabilities,” Newsom said at the time.

AB 1709 prevents socmed platforms from being able to employ features like recommendation algorithms, infinite scroll, and video autoplay on accounts of users under the age of 16. Because of how Google, TikTok, and Meta have designed their platforms, it effectively bans kids under 16 from having accounts at all on YouTube, TikTok, Instagram, and Facebook.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

According to Reuters, the three big tech companies all said (in separate lawsuits) that California is wrongly forcing them to use a state-mandated method of delivering content to children, instead of leaving things up to parents’ discretion.

Their lawsuits claim violations of Section 230 (aka the “safe harbor” law that lets platforms off the liability hook for anything their users post) and First Amendment free speech protections.

“Parents ‌are best ⁠positioned to make the right decisions for their families,” Google spokesperson Jose Castaneda told Reuters. “Overly broad laws like this actually break the systems we’ve built to help kids find high-quality, age-appropriate videos.”

In its suit, TikTok said protected speech “cannot be suppressed solely to protect the young from ideas or images that a legislative body thinks is unsuitable.”

Meta’s suit, meanwhile, said California doesn’t have the right to override its editorial standards.

“We are challenging this law because it violates the First Amendment by letting the government dictate how ideas are organized, displayed or shared with Californians,” a Meta spokesperson told Courthouse News. “Under the law, we would be forced to either remove features that keep teens’ experiences age-appropriate, or cut thousands of California teens off from family, friends and their communities.”

TikTok’s suit said ​protected speech “cannot be suppressed solely to ​protect the young from ideas ⁠or images that a legislative body thinks unsuitable.”

“AB 1709 is a blatantly unconstitutional restriction on free expression that erodes the long-standing and protected practice of curating content based on user preferences, which book stores, TV networks and streaming services all use,” a TikTok spokesperson added. “Beyond disregarding real progress in making teens safer online, this law limits free speech and takes away parents’ rights to determine what’s appropriate for their kids.”

The defendant in all three suits is California Attorney General Rob Bonta; all three suits ask the court for an injunction that would stop him from enforcing AB 1709 against them.

Bonta’s office said it’s reviewing the suits, and that he is “committed to defending California’s commonsense laws to protect kids online.”

How Boeing Losing To Lockheed's C-5 Galaxy Gave The World The 747

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Chris earned a Master’s degree in Defense and Strategic Studies (Highest Honors) from the University of Texas at El Paso.

His graduate work concentrated on Global and National Security, Low-Intensity Conflict, Modern Warfare, and US grand strategy. He also undertook extensive regional studies, analyzing how key regions integrate into the broader US security architecture.

This rigorous program provided him with a comprehensive understanding of U.S. military operations, global strategy, and weapons systems. Additionally, he gained a strong appreciation for great power competition.—an increasingly central force shaping international relations and conflict for the foreseeable future.

Born in an era of Cold War competition and growing military airlift needs, the Boeing 747 became one of the most recognizable and influential aircraft in aviation history. Since entering commercial service in 1970, the jumbo jet has transformed passenger and cargo transport worldwide.

Spanning five generations, the 747 evolved alongside the aviation industry, culminating in the technologically advanced 747-8. Yet, its arrival coincided with a shift toward smaller, more fuel-efficient twin-engine aircraft, signaling the beginning of the end for the iconic four-engine airliner.

As a doctrine, Flexible Response was initiated by the Kennedy administration to confront the challenges posed by the Soviet Union on various fronts. As opposed to the doctrine of Massive Retaliation, which relied heavily on the threat of massive nuclear counterstrikes, Flexible Response, introduced in 1961, provided the president with enhanced options when confronting a crisis, ranging from greater nonmilitary to military responses. These options included diplomatic, political, and economic measures, as well as strengthening the military's ability to respond quickly wherever Soviet pressure emerged.

Regarded as the architect of Flexible Response, General Maxwell Taylor advocated for five central pillars concerning the military aspect of the new doctrine. According to the US Army War College Quarterly, Parameters, the second pillar called for the military to be revitalized to conduct warfare at levels below general or all-out war. The third pillar emphasized the need for a continental US-based, active-duty force in a high state of readiness. These personnel would be ready at a moment's notice for rapid deployment overseas to support forward-operating US military forces or allies.

Crucially, for the development of what would become the 747 was the fourth pillar, which highlighted the need to "...modernize and increase the size of air and sealift forces to provide a major capability for the rapid deployment of the active duty forces in the United States, and for resupply of all committed forces."

Moving the required military personnel to a contested region requires speed. If this were done solely by sea, a critical opportunity to halt a Soviet or Soviet-sponsored force could be missed. To this point, the United States Air Force (USAF) stated that "President John F. Kennedy, in his first defense message to Congress in March 1961, announced the buildup of the airlift forces to support flexible response..."

Following this announcement, President Kennedy endorsed the immediate development of the Cargo Experimental-Heavy Logistics System (CX-HLS) program. The five competitors were Lockheed-Georgia Company, Boeing, Douglas Aircraft Company, General Dynamics, and Martin Marietta.

Some of the best military designs emerged during the Cold War.

The future of providing the USAF and, by extension, the US military with timely logistics and surge capacity in a geographic region under Soviet duress was a major priority. On December 22, 1964, Secretary of Defense Robert S. McNamara announced approval of the plan to develop a new outsized logistics aircraft, moving the CX-HLS competition forward.

By May of that year, proposals for the aircraft had been received from the competing companies. After a down-select, Boeing, Douglas, and Lockheed Martin each received one-year study contracts for their airframe designs, while General Electric and Pratt & Whitney received contracts for engine development.

According to a December 1964 issue of Boeing Magazine, the company said competitive subcontracting for the project could total more than $1 billion (£745.7 million), equivalent to about $10.87 billion (£8.1 billion) today. Furthermore, the magazine explained, "The airplane could be the largest ever built. More than 11,000 suppliers in the United States and Canada will participate in the program if Boeing wins the contract."

The Journal of Aircraft and Spacecraft Technology summarized the competition.

"The Air Force considered Boeing's design better than the Lockheed design, although Lockheed's proposal was the lowest total cost bid. Lockheed was selected the winner in September 1965, then awarded a contract in December 1965. General Electric's engine design was selected in August 1965 for the new transport..."

On December 17, 1969, the first C-5A Galaxy was delivered to the 443d Military Airlift Wing, located at Altus Air Force Base, Oklahoma. Today, the USAF maintains a fleet of 52 C-5s. The aircraft has a maximum payload of 281,001 lb (127,460 kg) and an unrefueled range of 5,524 statute miles (8,890 km), according to the USAF. The aircraft would remain a mainstay of America's strategic airlift capability until the Lockheed Martin C-17 entered service in 1995.

The story behind America's flying colossus.

Although Boeing suffered a stinging loss in its bid for the major Air Force contract, the company bounced back at a critical juncture in commercial aviation. According to R. A. Davis, a former Chief Project Engineer for the Boeing 747, "Revenue passenger-miles (less than 50 billion in 1955) had more than tripled by 1965 on a rapidly rising trend. Air travel was forecast to reach at least 500 billion passenger-miles by the mid-1970s, and almost a tenfold increase in air cargo was predicted for the same period."

Furthermore, at the time, passenger traffic was expected to approximately double every five years. With airports already congested, adding more aircraft to accommodate traffic growth would only compound the problem.

Elaborating on this issue, Davis explained that if the congestion problem went unresolved, "By 1975, about 20 million departures of 707-sized aircraft would have to be scheduled each year to meet the predicted world demand. By contrast, if airplanes of 350-400 passenger capability were phased into the long-haul routes, it was estimated that annual departures could be reduced to about 12 million." Clearly, manufacturing larger aircraft appeared to be a promising way forward.

Tony Douglas of startup Riyadh Air says paper boarding passes will not even be an option with the airline.

Taking on the challenge, in 1965, Boeing tasked its Chief Engineer, Joe Sutter — widely known as the Father of the 747 — with reviewing the aircraft's design and reconfiguring it for commercial use. In doing so, the Northwest Airlines History Center states that the "...rear ramp and high wing were traded for a conventional layout, but the option for nose loading was retained — pushing the cockpit above the main deck and creating the distinctive “hump” and upper-deck cabin."

Incredibly, the aircraft went from the drawing board to a completed aircraft in a remarkably short period. In a piece by Airline Reporter, Mr. Sutter stated that "...Pan Am placed its first order in 1966, and we rolled out the first aircraft in 1968, with the first flight in 1969, followed by entry into service in early 1970.” During its first year of service, the 747-100 ushered in a new era of wide-body transportation. Within that short period of time, Mr. Davis stated that the 747 fleet carried over 7 million passengers, demonstrating the aircraft's immediate demand and success as a trusted passenger carrier.

Nearly 15 years after entering service, Davis stated that the 747 fleet expanded to more than 670 airframes. Together, these aircraft had flown more than 19 million revenue hours, traveled over 9.5 billion miles, and transported more than 670 million passengers. Furthermore, by around 1986, Boeing was preparing to continue its remarkable commercial success with the introduction of the 747-400. At the time, the aircraft was expected to deliver greater range, improved fuel economy, and lower operating costs, with projected fuel consumption reductions of 11 to 15%.

The impact of the 737-300, and how the 737's role within Boeing'slineup and the market evolved.

Spanning five generations and 25 manufactured variants, the 747 has been flying domestic and international routes for 56 years, an incredible run. The final passenger model in this iconic aircraft family is the 747-8i, which is optimized for comfortable passenger travel; it entered commercial service on June 1, 2012.

Boeing says the 48-aircraft variant can accommodate a total of 467 passengers. The upper deck can seat 50 Business Class passengers, while First Class accommodates 24 passengers on the main deck, alongside a second Business Class section with seating for 37. The large Economy Class section seats 356 passengers. The 747-8i can also accommodate up to 38 LD-1 cargo containers across its lower-deck cargo holds.

The 747-8i also incorporates several significant technological improvements over the 747-400. Its fuselage was extended by 18.3 feet, adding passenger capacity, while a redesigned wing, improved aerodynamics, and four General Electric GEnx-2B engines improved fuel efficiency and performance. The aircraft also featured a more modern passenger cabin, with larger windows, LED lighting, and an updated interior design. Despite retaining the distinctive appearance and four-engine configuration of its predecessors, the 747-8i represented the most technologically advanced passenger version of the iconic jumbo jet.

Lufthansa was the first airline to purchase the 747-8i and ultimately operated 19 of the aircraft, making it the largest operator of the passenger variant worldwide. Korean Air and Air China also acquired smaller numbers. While the 747-8i was an incredible aircraft, it entered the market as airlines increasingly turned toward smaller, twin-engine aircraft offering greater fuel efficiency and range, such as the Boeing 777. These aircraft could operate many of the same routes without requiring airlines to fill more than 400 seats to make each flight profitable.

The MAX 8-200 is a special edition of the MAX 8 developed for ultra-low-cost carriers that seek to maximize the workhorse's seating capacity.

From its origins in an era of Cold War strategic competition to its role in transforming global aviation, the 747 has enjoyed an extraordinary legacy. The aircraft helped redefine long-haul passenger travel, expanded the possibilities of commercial air cargo, and demonstrated the advantages of moving more people and freight in a single flight.

Although changing economics and advances in twin-engine aircraft ultimately ended production, the 747 remains an enduring symbol of engineering ambition. Its influence on commercial aviation, military logistics, and aircraft design ensures that the Queen of the Skies will remain an important part of aviation history.

Nasdaq sell-off batters Asian chip stocks as SK Hynix, Samsung, Softbank, TSMC tumble

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Asian technology stocks tumbled Friday, tracking a sharp sell-off on Wall Street overnight that sent the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite into correction territory as renewed concerns over artificial intelligence spending rattled investor sentiment.

South Korean chipmaker SK Hynix plunged over 8%, while Samsung Electronics shed 7%. LG Innotek and LG Display fell 10% and 7.7%, respectively, as selling pressure spread across the country's technology sector.

The rout came as investors worried that Democrats' sweeping victory in the U.S. midterm elections could lead to tighter restrictions on data-center development, potentially slowing demand for AI chips and related infrastructure.

In Japan, semiconductor-related stocks also fell, with chip-testing equipment maker Advantest plunging 12.2%, while Tokyo Electron slid 7.3%. Technology investment giant SoftBank Group tumbled 11.6%.

Shares of memory chipmaker Kioxia Holdings fell nearly 10%, while Renesas Electronics declined 7.5%.

In Taiwan, semiconductor stocks also came under heavy selling pressure, with contract chipmaker TSMC sliding 9.6%. Electronics manufacturers Hon Hai Precision Industry, also known as Foxconn, and Foxconn Technology fell 9.4% and 9.1%, respectively, as the regional technology rout deepened.

China was also caught in the regional sell-off, with the tech-heavy STAR Market and the ChiNext Artificial Intelligence Index both tumbling more than 4%.

The declines followed a brutal session on Wall Street, where the Nasdaq Composite sank 5.5%, amid mounting concerns over the sustainability of the AI investment boom and returns on massive data-center spending. It closed more than 10% below its recent peak. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index plunged 12.2%.

The sell-off in Asian equities has been exacerbated by investors unwinding positions, stop-loss selling and heightened volatility, Timothy Moe, chief Asia Pacific equity strategist at Goldman Sachs, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" on Friday.He added that potential political opposition to U.S. data center construction may affect demand and profits for Asian AI hardware suppliers.

"If there's concern about the speed and magnitude of buildout in the U.S., then obviously that's going to put all that into question," he said. "The market's tendency is always to shoot first and ask questions later. I think that's exactly what's happening now."

Report: iPad shipments drop 27.5% as global tablet market plunges in Q3

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Despite global iPad shipments dropping 27.5% year-over-year in the third calendar quarter, Apple maintained its lead in the tablet market, according to a new report released today. Here are the details.

According to a new report published by Omdia (via MacMagazine), Apple shipped 11,5 million iPads during Q3 2026, accounting for 36% of all shipments.

Compared to Q3 2025, that was a 27.5% drop in shipments, and a 2.2 percentage-point decline in market share.

For context, the global tablet market saw a 23% year-over-year drop, with total shipments going from 41.8 million to 32.2 million units. Omdia noted this was the lowest level in nearly three years.

As for Apple’s competitors, Samsung remained in second place with 16.9% market share, followed by Lenovo with 12.7%, Xiaomi with 7.3%, and Huawei with 5.7%.

When it comes to shipment growth, Lenovo was the only company among the top five to post a positive result, up 11.9% year over year. Huawei saw the steepest decline at 43%, followed by Apple at 27.5%, Samsung at 21.7%, and Xiaomi at 10.6%.

Commenting on the results, Omdia Research Manager Himani Mukka said supply constraints are “limiting vendors’ ability to deliver meaningful specification upgrades, leaving fewer compelling differences between new models and their predecessors.”

“As a result,” she added, “consumers are increasingly expected to delay purchases until more substantial improvements become available.”

Both clubs are expected to monitor Tavernier’s progress before deciding whether to make a formal approach during the mid-season window.

Any club hoping to sign Bournemouth’s talisman is likely to have to put forward a sizeable offer to secure a mid-season deal.

Trump's arch gets a late-night green light. Will a judge stop construction?

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Demonstrators stand at Memorial Circle to protest President Trump's proposed arch on Thursday night as a federal panel heard hours of testimony from concerned citizens. It voted to approve the structure. Tom Brenner/Getty Images hide caption

A federal judge blocked construction of President Trump's arch early Friday morning, stopping work the administration had hoped to begin immediately.

Just before midnight, District Court Judge Tanya Chutkan issued an order barring the government from any construction work for 14 days, granting a request filed days earlier by the governments of D.C. and Virginia.

She laid out her reasoning in a 33-page opinion, in which she scrutinized the administration's competing justifications for the arch — particularly its sudden emphasis on national security — and agreed that the local governments are likely to be harmed by its construction. The 250-foot arch would stand in Memorial Circle, a traffic island on the bridge linking the Lincoln Memorial to Arlington National Cemetery.

Trump administration officials have said work would take place 20 hours a day, year-round, for two to three years. In addition to questioning the arch's legality, D.C. and Virginia argue the construction would cause all sorts of financial and logistical disruptions.

"The threatened harms will materialize as soon as construction begins, in part because the traffic disruptions will occur immediately," Chutkan agreed.

Chutkan's ruling came less than two hours after a federal planning commission packed with presidential allies granted final approval to the arch in an 8-4 vote. The National Capital Planning Commission heard nearly eight hours of impassioned pleas from veterans, lawmakers, residents and concerned citizens opposed to the structure.

The Trump administration had previously told the court it could begin excavation work that same day, despite lacking authorization from Congress and an expanding legal challenge.

A group of Vietnam War veterans and an architectural historian sued the government earlier this year, arguing that an arch is unlawful without Congressional approval, which the administration has not sought. The Democratic governments of D.C. and Virginia jointly filed a similar lawsuit last weekend.

Chutkan consolidated the lawsuits and asked the government to notify the court within an hour of commission approval, setting the stage for an 11th-hour ruling.

Thursday started with a protest on Memorial Circle, where dozens of people spoke against the arch. The grass underfoot was already marked with white paint. Lawyers for the government told the court in a midday filing that the lines were painted on Saturday by its designated construction firm, seemingly without authorization from the National Park Service.

The commission meeting began at 1 p.m. It was held virtually because of the high volume of public testimony: 131 people were signed up to speak.

Just minutes into public testimony, the livestream crashed, lengthening the meeting by about an hour until it was restored. Over the course of nearly nine hours, commissioners heard from dozens of veterans and their relatives, Democratic lawmakers, historic preservation experts and concerned citizens from D.C. and beyond.

Among their many concerns, critics said the structure would obstruct an important view, insult service members buried on the hallowed ground of nearby Arlington National Cemetery and add a layer of risk to the notoriously complicated D.C. airspace. They have questions about its legality and its cost, which the administration has not confirmed.

Many see the arch as part of Trump's unilateral effort to memorialize himself in the nation's capital — and described Democrats' midterm sweep as a referendum against it.

"Leaving aside all of the legitimate concerns and challenges about the required approvals, funding, land use, single-source bidding, and peer review, the American people spoke this past Tuesday at the ballot box," said former D.C. resident Emi Yamauchi. "They said enough vanity projects, enough corruption, enough Reflecting Pool travesties."

Only a handful of speakers offered support for the arch, including Republican Reps. Andrew Clyde of Georgia and Brian Mast of Florida, both of whom are veterans.

Much of the night's testimony was heartfelt and personal: Many speakers had family members buried — or plans for their own future burials — at Arlington National Cemetery. Several implored the commissioners not to ruin their reputations by recording their support for the arch.

"[These are] warnings about the threat your own behavior poses to your political power, your moral authority, the legacy you leave to your descendants, and your eternal souls," said Stephen Eubank, some seven hours into the public testimony.

As the night wore on, a few speakers even questioned whether the commissioners — who were not shown on screen — were even listening. Many suggested the approval was a foregone conclusion, noting the political makeup of the commission and the fact that Trump-appointed Chair Mark Paoletta and several other members had expressed their support for the arch at the start of the meeting.

The commission started deliberating just before 10 p.m. The discussion lasted less than an hour.

The commissioners who spoke first were Trump allies and appointees. They thanked the speakers for taking the time to share their views, but — in brief comments — expressed their support for moving forward. They continued to cast the arch as a tribute to the nation and those who served, despite the broad pushback from individual veterans and veterans' groups.

The Trump allies dismissed concerns that the arch would violate D.C.'s restrictions on building heights as well as federal environmental and historic preservation laws. They maintained that Congress authorized the project when it approved a pair of 166-foot columns on Memorial Circle in 1925, which were never built.

And they pointed to recent claims by Trump and Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth that the monument will enhance national security — even though officials at the meeting confirmed that no such analysis or details were included in the final submission. In fact, Interior Department official Greg Wischer said "specific national security functions have been filed under seal" in court but that the National Park Service "has not been privy to any national defense preparations."

The most vocal criticism of the arch came from D.C. Council Chair Phil Mendelson, a Democrat who sits on the council. He asked questions about the arch's purpose — military and otherwise — earlier in the meeting and said, during deliberations, that he was dissatisfied with the government's contradictory answers.

"We cannot approve final site and building plans until we have the final site and building plan," Mendelson said. He introduced a motion to postpone the vote until those plans could be submitted, or until the commission's next meeting in early December. The motion failed by a vote of 4 to 8.

Vice Chair Stuart Levenbach, a Trump appointee, expressed concerns about delaying the vote because the "project proponent is under a time constraint and wants the decision today," suggesting the government wants to "commence building very quickly."

Within minutes, the commission had given the arch its green light. The Trump administration notified the court within half an hour. By Friday, that light had turned red — at least temporarily.

"While this decision prevents Donald Trump from immediately implementing his plans to create a monument to his own ego, the courts and Congress must put in place permanent safeguards to stop the Trump Administration from violating the law and proceeding with this project,"  Rep. Don Beyer, a Virginia Democrat, said in a statement.

Arch critics look to the courts and Congress for relief

Historic preservation groups wasted no time slamming both the process and the outcome.

Brent Leggs, the president and CEO of the National Trust for Historic Preservation, called the night's vote a "sham."

"It undermines the democratic process, violates federal statute, and insults our nation's veterans who have made the ultimate sacrifice," he said in a statement. "From the start, this Arch project has been driven by an artificial sense of urgency, while public opposition has been brushed aside."

Jimi Shaughnessy, veterans program manager for the National Parks Conservation Association, said in a statement that the organization is disappointed but not surprised. He vowed to "bring this to members of Congress, to ensure this arch is never built. Not here. Not ever."

Many Democratic lawmakers have vowed to stop — and investigate — the arch after they regain a Congressional majority in January.

Robert Weissman, the co-president of Public Citizen — the consumer rights nonprofit representing the veterans in their lawsuit — told NPR on Thursday that Congress doesn't need to act to stop the arch, since it never actively approved it. That decision, he said, will be up to the courts.

But, he added: "It would be fine if Congress takes additional measures to block construction, since we've seen this lawless administration circumvent the law and court orders so frequently."

Virginia Rep. Don Beyer has introduced a bill that would prohibit and defund the arch, and said Thursday he expects the number of co-sponsors to grow from 80 to at least 200 in the new year.

And Rep. Jared Huffman, D-Calif., the ranking Democrat on the House Natural Resources Committee, warned commissioners that companies and "officials working to advance a project not authorized by Congress will put themselves at legal risk."

He argued that the project violates federal environmental and historic preservation laws, which fall within the jurisdiction of the House committee he expects to soon lead.

"I urge all officials and firms involved with this illegal project to consider the legal hazards they may be facing by advancing it, because, in case you hadn't heard, we'll soon have a new Congress that takes the rule of law very seriously," Huffman said.

NPR does not offer or accept money for coverage or interviews. Anyone who makes such an offer or request is not an NPR employee and does not represent NPR.

A Complete Guide To The Extra Life 2026 MTG Secret Lair Bundle

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Not every Magic: The Gathering Secret Lair bundle is limited in availability; Wizards of the Coast often releases philanthropic bundles on a print-to-demand basis, and this year's Extra Life bundles are part of this venture.

This year's Extra Life bundle, Rise of the Polygons, includes five cards with throwback artwork featuring old-school video game polygon art reminiscent of games like Final Fantasy VII. The idea behind the cards is that we're following a "young pyromancer" who looks a lot like Chandra, as they level up to take on the Balefire Dragon. MTG Arena is also releasing digital bundles alongside this physical drop, which we'll detail below, and they also support the Extra Life charity.

The Rise of the Polygons Extra Life Secret Lair will be on sale from November 16, 2026, to November 30, 2026. You will also have the chance to grab the bundle live at MagicCon Atlanta, although it will only be available in limited quantities each day. You can also donate to Extra Life at MagicCon Atlanta to earn rewards, where a $40 donation gets you a mystery Collector Booster while a $20 donation gets you a Loot pin.

This bundle contains five cards, although one is an Elemental token. The good news is that the bundle still has strong value elsewhere, but since 50% of the proceeds from each one go to the Children's Miracle Network, potential profits should be the last thing on your mind.

Here are all the cards from the Rise of the Polygons bundle, as well as the current cheapest and most expensive versions of those cards (using TCGplayer market value).

The price of this bundle is $39.99 for the nonfoil and $49.99 for the foil variant, meaning it's a $10 increase across the board, but we assume this is to ensure the donation made per bundle is adequate while Wizards of the Coasts' costs are still covered, too.

Assembling your Commander decks is a breeze with these five essential tools to make deck building easy.

Each of the cards will be available in MTG Arena as card sleeves, while an additional set of three card sleeves is also available, displaying cutesy versions of Chandra, Ajani, and a character called Gizmo.

Finally, there is an emote showing Gizmo doing a cute dance. All three sets of items must be purchased separately, with the Secret Lair card sleeves costing 3,000 gems, the Extra Life sleeves costing 1,800 gems, and the Gizmo sticker costing 800 gems.

A final drop from this collab is the Dragon Shield cosmetics, which include Ajani and Chandra card sleeves and a playmat featuring both Planeswalkers.

Packs of the sleeves cost $20, while the playmat costs $25, and $10 from every purchase is being donated directly to Extra Life. These products are exclusive to MagicCon Atlanta.

Hyundai Motor Group Outlines Future Mobility Technology Roadmap at FISITA World Mobility Summit 2026

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

HWASEONG, Korea — Hyundai Motor Group (the Group) is co-hosting the FISITA World Mobility Summit 2026 with the International Federation of Automotive Engineering Societies (FISITA). The two-day summit is taking place Nov. 4–5 at Rolling Hills Hotel in Hwaseong, Korea.

Founded in 1948, FISITA is a global organization that brings together automotive engineering societies and industry experts to promote knowledge exchange and technological innovation in automotive engineering. It holds the World Mobility Summit every two years, bringing together executives and technology leaders from global mobility companies to discuss industry challenges and future technology directions, such as artificial intelligence (AI) and vehicle electrification.

This year’s summit is the first to be held in Korea. Chang Hwan Kim, Executive Vice President and Head of the Electrification Energy Solutions Tech Unit at Hyundai Motor Group, is the first Korean to serve as FISITA President.

“Hyundai Motor Group will continue to strengthen its competitiveness in key future mobility technologies and continue to participate in technology exchange and collaboration across the global automotive industry to contribute to the development of the future mobility sector.” –Chang Hwan Kim, Executive Vice President and Head of the Electrification Energy Solutions Tech Unit at Hyundai Motor Group

On the first day, Manfred Harrer, President and Head of R&D Division at Hyundai Motor Group, delivered a keynote address introducing the Group’s electrification strategy. It encompasses battery electric vehicles (BEVs), hybrid electric vehicles (HEVs), extended-range electric vehicles (EREVs) and fuel cell electric vehicles (FCEVs). Harrer described the Group’s technology development roadmap for responding flexibly to market changes and customer needs.

Harrer also emphasized strengthening future vehicle development capabilities by combining engineering technology with AI-based digital capabilities and connecting the strengths of different technologies to pursue a sustainable mobility future.

In his opening remarks, Kim said that the automotive industry is undergoing an unprecedented technological transition. He emphasized that diverse technology approaches and stronger global collaboration are important to the advancement of future mobility.

Under the theme “Embracing the Complexities of Mobility,” the summit is addressing key technologies and challenges facing the automotive industry, including software-defined vehicles (SDVs), AI, autonomous driving, batteries and energy infrastructure.

Executives and technology leaders from across the global automotive industry are in attendance, sharing perspectives on key technology challenges and future development directions for the mobility industry.

Keynotes and panel discussions are examining major changes in the automotive industry, including advances in AI and software, the evolution of autonomous driving technology and the diversification of electrification technologies. Participants are also discussing technology directions and ways for the industry to collaborate in response to these shifts.

Major automakers are presenting vehicle and technology exhibits during the summit. The Group is displaying the Genesis GV90 and Hyundai TUCSON, along with a battery pack, hydrogen tank and robotics platform.

Rosamund Pike Starring in Body Horror Film ‘Feed’ for ‘Star Wars: The Acolyte’ Creator Leslye Headland

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Rosamund Pike will star in and produce “Feed,” a body horror film from “Russian Doll” co-creator and “Star Wars: The Acolyte” creator Leslye Headland.

The film follows a desperate mother who discovers an obscure collagen supplement that promises to transform her tween gymnast daughter into an influencer. Fame hits fast, but as her daughter goes viral, she undergoes a terrifying transformation. “Feed” was inspired by a 2022 Finnish horror film, “Hatching.” Daniella Isaacs (“People Who Knew Me”) wrote the script.

Headland made her feature debut in 2012 with an adaptation of her play, “Bachelorette.” In 2015, she wrote and directed “Sleeping With Other People.” Both premiered at the Sundance Film Festival. Related Stories Letterboxd Finalizing $330 Million Sale to The New York Times Why ASMR Invaded the Soundscape of ‘La Bola Negra’

Pike was nominated for an Oscar for “Gone Girl.” Her other credits include “Saltburn,” “An Education,” and the James Bond film, “Die Another Day.” She won an Olivier Award for “Inter Alia,” a role she is reprising this fall on Broadway.

“Long have I wanted to make a film that plays anarchically with our obsession with social media and self-improvement, our desire for fame without notable admirable skill and, let’s put it out there, the monstrosity of modern motherhood,” Pike said in a statement. “‘Feed’ deals with all these things. It tracks a Momfluencer, Erin, bent on her daughter’s domination of the digital space. Erin believes that success here will protect her daughter from the horrors of our fickle world… won’t it? ‘Feed’ examines appetite. The dark appetite of online communities; the appetite of a social media landscape that monetizes youth and beauty; the appetite of a mother for her daughter’s success; and the appetite of a teenage girl, hungry for fame, self-empowerment and dominance in her relationship with her single mother. ‘Feed’ is film that plays in the genre space, and plays into our current fears, as parents, as citizens, of the digital world.”

Headland added: “Daniella’s script is audacious, disturbing and deeply human, and Rosamund is among the boldest actors working today. I couldn’t ask for better partners to take on the darker corners of this story and I feel incredibly lucky to be making this film with two women whose work I admire so much.”

The Veterans is overseeing the international sales rights, with CAA Media Finance tackling the U.S. sales. Talks will kick off next week at the American Film Market.

Pike will produce through her Primitive Streak banner, alongside Shelley Browning for Magnolia Entertainment, Steven Schneider for Spooky Pictures, Ara Keshishian and Petr Jakl for ZQ Entertainment, and Sam Ritzenberg for Agile Films. ZQ Entertainment financed the development of the script.

Pike is repped by CAA, United Agents and Magnolia Entertainment; Headland is repped by CAA and Sugar23; Isaacs is repped by Curtis Brown Group.

God of War Ragnarok is PlayStation's most successful game this generation, says market analyst

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

God of War Ragnarok attracted accolades like magnets in 2022 even though it lost the main Game of the Year title to Elden Ring, and now here we are four years later handing the game yet another achievement. According to a prominent industry market analyst, God of War Ragnarok is the top-grossing first-party PlayStation game of this console generation.

Rhys Elliott, head of market analysis at Alinea Analytics, took to Twitter to share some new data about God of War Ragnarok's lifetime sales. According to that data, the game has sold 21.6 million copies across PlayStation and Steam, with Steam sales accounting for just 1.6 million of those copies sold. That amounts to $1.4 billion in gross revenue, catapulting it to the top of PlayStation's slate of first-party games for this generation.

As if reading your thoughts, Elliott knows you're wondering how God of War Ragnarok beats out a juggernaut like Marvel's Spider-Man 2, which obviously has massive crossover potential with one of the biggest fandoms in film, TV, and comics. Elliott notes that Marvel's Spider-Man 2 is a PS5 console exclusive, meaning it's not available on PS4 like God of War Ragnarok. That lowers its install base hugely. Also, God of War Ragnarok came out about a year before Marvel's Spider-Man 2, giving it more time to accumulate those sales. Still, God of War Ragnarok wears the PlayStation crown this generation.

"It's another PlayStation evergreen seller, too," Elliott says. "This year, Ragnarok has already sold 1.1M copies, generating nearly $60M (68% on PlayStation), outperforming new 2026 titles like Gears of War E-Day and Saros."

"Overall, Ragnarok has been accessed by 6.7M PlayStation Plus players (it was added to the Extra tier about two years ago). So it's performed that well this year DESPITE subscription cannibalisation. That's the pull of God of War IP."

Speaking of the God of War IP, there's a TV adaptation in the works at Prime Video starring Dave Bautista, and although there's no release date just yet, Elliott predicts the series "is going to be massive - bigger than Fallout, I reckon."

Naturally, achieving that sales milestone puts a lot of pressure on Sony Santa Monica to really deliver with God of War Laufey, the next game in its Norse series of God of War games. Notably, God of War Laufey marks a major departure for the series by switching playable characters from Kratos and Atreus to Laufey, Kratos's late wife and Atreus's mother. Having died just before the events of 2018's God of War, Laufey is forced to navigate a world inhabited by fallen gods, which is about as chaotic and violent as you'd expect.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

God of War Laufey is releasing May 20, 2027 after a recent delay.

God of War Laufey is exactly how I want the series' combat to evolve, from the return of aerial combos to hitting dudes so hard their souls fly out.

Gate boasts 104% cold reserve coverage, but separate snapshot dates raise red flags

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Its new hot-wallet loss test uses separate measurement bases, while independent verification of current PoSEC data remains in progress.

Crypto exchange Gate has introduced Proof of Security, a disclosure framework that proves its cold assets cover customer balances if it lost all its hot-wallet assets.

Its Nov. 5 announcement reports 104.58% cold-storage coverage, using a storage snapshot taken at 00:00 UTC on Nov. 4.

The published percentage compares asset shares from different snapshots, rather than establishing cold assets and customer balances on a single common date. Gate discloses that limitation, so customers need more than the headline ratio to verify the full-backing claim.

Gate's PoSEC methodology compares an 88.26% cold-storage share with an 84.39% user-asset share. The remaining shares are 11.74% in hot wallets and 15.61% owned by the platform.

Cold storage is measured against cold assets plus hot-wallet assets in the storage snapshot, while user assets are measured against platform reserves in the separate Proof of Reserves snapshot. The FAQ defines the coverage calculation as the first share divided by the second.

The page explicitly says the snapshots have different totals and that intervening price changes and fund movements are not reflected.

Same-date backing needs aligned balances. Gate’s reported percentage combines shares from different snapshot totals.

The reported 104.58% share comparison alone cannot establish backing at one common date.

Different snapshot dates and totals; intervening price changes and fund movements are excluded. The earlier PoR reference is not separately dated in the chart. This does not establish insolvency or losses.

Rings normalize each reported share to its own 100% total; they do not compare absolute dollar values. Arc lengths use the displayed percentages. 88.26+11.74=100 and 84.39+15.61=100. The 104.58% result is Gate-reported, not recomputed from rounded display inputs.

Different snapshot dates and totals; intervening price changes and fund movements are excluded. The earlier PoR reference is not separately dated in the chart. This does not establish insolvency or losses.

Gate's Oct. 21 disclosure reports roughly $8.7 billion in reserves and $7.3 billion in user net assets as of Oct. 18, with a 118.49% overall reserve ratio.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The PoSEC chart does not separately date its earlier user-asset reference, and the October dollar totals cannot be combined with November storage percentages to present a measured dollar buffer.

A direct test of backing after a hot-wallet loss would compare surviving cold assets with customer balances at the same time, using compatible valuations and the same reporting perimeter.

The disclosure leaves a gap in demonstrating common-date coverage, as it doesn't establish either insolvency or customer losses.

Gate's definition includes digital assets in cold wallets and traditional assets, such as stocks and US Treasuries, held by independent custodians.

The exchange says qualifying assets must sit outside routine deposit and withdrawal operations, be offline or independently custodied, require multiparty transfer authorization, and be verifiable. It also explains that off-chain traditional assets can make reported cold storage exceed balances visible in blockchain cold wallets.

As of Nov. 5, the PoSEC page lists independent third-party verification as in progress. Gate says the review covers asset authenticity and control, valuation and reporting scope, and the coverage calculation.

Gate's PoSEC FAQ says individual wallet addresses are currently withheld for security reasons, making the promised independent report particularly relevant to public scrutiny.

That review should be distinguished from the older audit shown on Gate's Proof of Reserves page. The page dates a Hacken implementation assessment to Jan. 3, 2024. Hacken describes that work as examining code, architecture, build processes, and vulnerabilities.

The new framework gives customers a concrete scenario to evaluate. The decisive next evidence is a common-date reconciliation of cold assets and customer balances, with valuation methods, entity scope, custody restrictions, and asset composition clear enough to assess the claim.

A completed review can show how those pieces fit together, while a comparison of percentages from different snapshots cannot do that by itself.

OpenAI is stepping back from NDAs for data center projects

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The ChatGPT maker won’t use NDAs with public officials as part of its new data center community commitments.

The ChatGPT maker won’t use NDAs with public officials as part of its new data center community commitments.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Following this week’s midterms where voters resoundingly voiced their opposition to data center expansion, OpenAI today announced new commitments to communities where it is working on data center projects, including that it won’t use non-disclosure agreements (NDAs) with public officials.

Many data center projects have come under scrutiny for relying on NDAs to hide discussions between developers and local officials. However, OpenAI says it is now allowing “only narrow protections covering legitimate security information, trade secrets, or proprietary or competitively sensitive information.” In October, Amazon announced it would no longer use NDAs with government agencies it works with on data center projects.

OpenAI’s additional commitments include paying “the full cost” of electricity it uses and the “infrastructure needed to serve us.” It will make opportunities for local communities by publishing the jobs it expects to create and by reporting on hiring. And every future “compute campus” (its rebranding phrase for data centers) the company owns and operates will have a “local compact” that includes the commitments and will reflect “local priorities,” OpenAI says.

Apple expected to bid for F1 streaming rights in more countries, says Formula 1 CEO

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

On the latest earnings call for F1’s parent company Liberty Media, the CEO of Formula 1 Stefano Domenicali said that both parties are happy with the US deal, and he expects that Apple will bid for rights in more countries as the opportunities become available.

F1 has previously expressed that they are not currently looking for one media partner to take global broadcast rights for the sport; they want a portfolio of deals, picking the best partners for each geographic market.

We have already seen this play out in the United Kingdom, with broadcaster Sky renewing its deal for F1 rights through 2034, seemingly locking Apple out of that region. So, despite the apparent demand, Apple will not be able to just walk in and take over the future of the sport’s worldwide distribution.

However, it seems the league is open to striking more deals with Apple in certain markets.

On the earnings call, in response to a question from an investor about Apple’s interest, Domenicali said “this is part of our discussion, if there are opportunities that can be in other markets, they will do it … they’re going to do it as soon as these opportunities will be open for them”.

Liberty Media CEO Derek Chang said they continue to think about what new innovation they can bring to the product side, and noted that “Apple as a potential bidder in other markets is helpful” for their business.

As a reminder, all Formula 1 races are available to watch live and on-demand with an Apple TV subscription, in the United States. The company recently launched a Dolby Atmos audio track for its F1 streams, as well as expanded support for Multiview on more platforms, including iPhone Duo.

Five9 buys contact center intelligence startup Level AI after posting narrow earnings beat

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Contact center company Five9 Inc. flexed its acquisitive muscles today as it announced a $325 million all-cash deal to buy an artificial intelligence-native rival called Level AI as it posted its third-quarter financial results.

The deal is expected to close in the fourth quarter, subject to customary closing conditions. Five9 is a leading player in the cloud contact center software industry, selling tools and services that enterprises use to manage their customer service interactions. Its web-based software spans traditional phone calls, video calls, emails, messaging, social media and other channels.

As for Level AI, it’s the developer of a contact center and customer experience intelligence platform that uses artificial intelligence to support human agents on calls and analyze, score and augment their interactions with the callers they serve. Its platform is designed to listen into customer service agent’s calls, so it can provide real-time guidance throughout and support workflow automation. The insights it generates can also be used to assist in agent coaching.

Level AI also develops autonomous AI agents that can automate some simpler customer inquiries and background operational tasks, freeing up human agents to focus on more complex customer issues. “Level AI’s AI applications help organizations improve agent performance, automate more customer interactions across voice and digital channels, and turn conversations into insights the whole organization can use,” Five9 said in a blog post announcing the deal. “Its applications are in production with enterprise customers today and handle more than 1 billion customer interactions a year.”

Five9 said it intends to continue offering Level AI’s applications as standalone tools for organizations that don’t want to shift their on-premises contact center operations to the cloud, also those who are using other cloud contact center platforms. However, it also plans to integrate the startup’s tools with its Genius CX Platform.

“As AI reshapes the customer experience landscape, we believe adding Level AI strengthens our competitive position, expands the customers we can serve, and deepens the AI talent behind our continuous innovation,” said Five9 Chief Executive Amit Mathradas.

Level AI founder and CEO Ashish Nagar said that by joining Five9, his company will be able to bring its applications and customer service-native AI models to thousands of new contact centers around the world, while allowing it to continue supporting its existing customers.

Investors don’t seem to have shared the CEOs’ enthusiasm for the deal, however, for Five9’s stock fell more than 23% in late trading after the deal was announced. That may be because of the cost of the acquisition. The $325 million cash outlay amounts to almost half of Five9’s current cash and marketable investments, which stood at $738 million at the end of the third quarter.

Five9’s results weren’t too bad. The company reported adjusted earnings of 82 cents per share on revenue of $323.3 million, surpassing Wall Street’s guidance of 80 cents per share in earnings and $318.3 million in sales. But it’s notable that the company’s full-year guidance excludes any contributions from the pending deal, and so its value hinges on Five9’s ability to rapidly integrate Level AI’s technology.

For the current quarter, Five9 said it’s expecting earnings of between 88 and 92 cents per share on sales of $328 million to $334 million. But that guidance was mixed. Wall Street is looking for significantly higher earnings of 99 cents per share and $330.7 million in revenue.

For the full year, Five9 said it’s looking for earnings of $3.17 to $3.21 per share on total sales of $1.27 billion. Wall Street wants to see higher earnings of $3.26 per share on similar revenue.

Messi deserves MLS MVP award, says Miami boss Gonzalez

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Inter Miami head coach Cristian 'Kily' Gonzalez believes Lionel Messi "deserves the MVP and also the Ballon d'Or" for his performances at 39 years of age.

Messi was on the scoresheet again last weekend as Miami defeated Eastern Conference rivals New England Revolution 2-1 on the road.

The win saw the Herons move back up to second in the East, one point ahead of the Revs, as they now prepare to host Charlotte FC on Decision Day.

Messi has scored more goals than any other player in MLS this season, with 26, while only Evander (17) and Miami team-mate Rodrigo De Paul (16) have provided more assists (15).

"I have no doubt that he's the best of all," Gonzalez said of Messi.

"None of them can compare to him when it comes to respect for his colleagues.

"Leo at his age and what he does, from my point of view, deserves the MVP and also the Ballon d'Or. I hope he can achieve it."

Messi's goal against New England also clinched Miami's spot in the 2027 Champions Cup.

"It makes me very happy for the players and for the club, we are on the side of contributing things," added Gonzalez.

"We are at a club that is in continuous growth, I have players committed to us. We needed to win and we challenged ourselves on what kind of team we wanted to be."

— Inter Miami CF (@InterMiamiCF) November 5, 2026

Charlotte are fifth in the East and already know they will face the Chicago Fire in the first round of the play-offs.

They thrashed Orlando City SC 4-0 last time out, but Henry Klesser, who scored in that win, is wary of Miami's strengths.

"Obviously a very difficult matchup," Kessler said. "They've got a lot of talented guys, so we'll have to be prepared, but I think it's going to take a team effort."

Messi failed to contribute to a goal in Miami's last home match, ending a run of 12 straight home starts with a goal contribution dating back to April.

Messi was directly involved in 23 goals (14 goals, nine assists) over that run.

Rodolfo Aloko and Abdoulaye Doucoure both came off the bench to score for Charlotte in their win over Orlando.

They are the third pair of team-mates to score as substitutes in a single match for Charlotte, and the first since Kerwin Vargas and Patrick Agyemang netted against New England in September 2024.

Miami are unbeaten in eight straight home matches in all competitions (W4 D4) after a three-match losing run in August.

The Herons have scored in 36 straight home matches in all competitions dating back to the Leagues Cup in 2025 at Chase Stadium, last failing to score at home against FC Cincinnati in July 2025.

Charlotte have posted 4-0 wins in each of their last two matches, over CF Montreal and Orlando, both at home. This is the 17th time in MLS history a team have won consecutive games by four or more goals, though no team have done so in three straight.

The Crown kept clean sheets in each of their last two meetings with Miami, including a scoreless draw in the last meeting in March. The only team to keep clean sheets in three straight regular-season meetings with Miami was Nashville SC in 2020-21.

The group was supposed to perform at Chicago’s United Center tonight, Nov. 5, and at Minneapolis’ Target Center on Saturday, Nov. 7. The news was shared on the venues’ respective social media pages before the group addressed the situation themselves on Instagram.

“The label has made the decision to postpone our shows in Chicago and Minneapolis as Megan is recovering from a concussion,” the group wrote in their Instagram Story on Thursday. “We are incredibly sorry to disappoint our fans. We know how much you’ve been looking forward to these shows, and that many of you have traveled long distances to be there. We don’t take that lightly, and we’re truly sorry for the disappointment.

“We’ll announce the rescheduled dates as soon as possible, so please hold on to your tickets,” they continued. “We can’t wait to see you in both cities again soon. Thank you for your continued love, support and understanding.”

A rep for Hybe did not immediately respond to Rolling Stone’s request for comment.

The two show postponements come on the heels of Katseye postponing their show in Detroit earlier this week, mere hours before they were set to take the stage at Little Caesars Arena. At the time, the group said it was due to Skiendiel being “unwell” with an unspecified illness. I Can’t Imagine Getting Into A Brawl At Disney World, But It Happened (& Now There’s A Lawsuit)

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

What happened to Disney theme parks being the happiest place on Earth?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Disney theme parks are supposed to be the happiest place on earth, where you spend time making memories with family and loved ones. That is, until someone gets into an all-out brawl in the line for Avatar Flight of Passage in Pandora – The World of Avatar inside Disney’s Animal Kingdom, and it results in a lawsuit. Apparently, the individuals involved had been arguing for the entirety of their 80-minute wait, and it finally reached a boiling point, leading to several people seemingly getting physical.

While I find it wild that this happened at all, what makes it even wilder is the fact that this is not the first time a brawl has taken place in a ride queue. Line cutting at Indiana Jones Adventure at Disneyland led to an incident when the person who was doing the cutting hit a man who said something about it, which incited a brawl and resulted in the attacked man sustaining a minor injury.

Pertaining to the fight in Pandora, a series of Reddit posts point to an unknown cause for the arguing between two groups, stating that it didn’t appear to have anything to do with cutting in line. Honestly, regardless of what sparked the fighting, that kind of behavior can’t and shouldn’t be tolerated, and apparently Morgan & Morgan feels the same way, as they’re suing Disney on behalf of a family that was struck several times.

In a statement to Florida Politics, Morgan & Morgan's founder John Morgan and attorney Christopher Ryan explained what reportedly happened to their clients. They also stated the family's expectation for a safe trip at Disney World:

Our clients were on a family vacation at Disney’s Animal Kingdom when they were confronted by a group of adults in line for Avatar Flight of Passage. The encounter allegedly escalated into a physical altercation in which our clients, including their teenage daughter, were struck several times. Families come to Disney expecting to be safe.

In the complaint, it's explained that this group "allegedly pushed Plaintiff and her family, climbed over queue barriers, and struck Plaintiff." It also states that the plaintiff sustained "bodily injuries and damages."

The lawsuit also accused employees of not monitoring the queue, failing to perform adequate crowd control, and not properly de-escalating the situation.

Millions of people visit Disney theme parks every year, and even though these stories of bad behavior tend to make headlines, it does involve a relatively very small number of individuals in comparison. That’s not to say any instance of violence is acceptable (it’s very much not okay). Whether it’s two families throwing fists at Magic Kingdom or a fistfight in Mickey’s Toontown at Disneyland, or even that scuffle over a Disney World photo op, this should just not be happening in any capacity.

In the past, viral videos of Disney World skirmishes tend to get a lot of attention. Lifetime bans from Disney Parks can be lifted, but it requires those banned to send letters of apology to request that the ban be lifted, and it’s very rare that the decision gets reversed.

Sometimes, lawsuits happen too, which is what it has come to after the brawl that broke out on Flight of Passage.

As Democrats look to next era, the veto and the filibuster await

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

As Democrats continue their victory lap after their big wins on election night, outside groups and incoming freshmen are already turning up the pressure — and laying out competing visions of what the next two years should look like.

For Senate Democratic leader Charles E. Schumer, it bears repeating that “the Democrats aren’t all exactly the same.”

“The most important lesson of this whole election, and one we all need to internalize if we’re going to truly build a durable majority, is populism and moderation are not mutually exclusive,” he said at a Center for American Progress Action Fund event on Thursday.

Voters are expecting results as Democrats claim a mandate to address costs and corruption. “Our majority will be measured against one test and one test only: Have we made your life better?” Schumer said.

He acknowledged, however, that their plans could run into a President Donald Trump-sized wall: the presidential veto.

“Will everything pass? Will Trump sign all of it into law? Knowing Trump’s and Republicans’  intransigence, of course not. But I will promise the American people: The Democratic Party will fight to make your life more affordable, your government more competent, your capital less corrupt, bill by bill, brick by brick, for as long as it takes for you to feel the difference,” Schumer said, hinting the road could be a long one.

But several progressive lawmakers and groups say the next Congress cannot be business as usual.

Indivisible, a progressive political organization that has described the Trump administration as a “fascistic clown show,” sent a memo to Senate Democratic offices Thursday morning urging them not to “squander” their momentum and to “fight at a level that matches the public’s demand for action.”

“Who leads the party in this era must be an open question, not a coronation. It’s time to define what the next era of the Democratic Party will be,” the memo reads.

The group has been encouraging voters to speak up and demand their senators hold “a contested leadership election, not a cleared field for Schumer.”

Meanwhile, Schumer has projected confidence he will keep his spot atop the caucus, especially after he helped pave the way for Tuesday’s blue wave. “I’ll be reelected as majority leader,” he said Wednesday. But the pressure campaign points to some tough math and harsh realities for the top Democrat — including some that mirror the woes of the outgoing Republican Majority Leader John Thune.

Among the demands outlined by Indivisible is one with a troubled recent history: “Breaking the filibuster on the first day of the new session.”

Democrats need to leverage “every procedural tool” if they “want the public to believe their campaign promises weren’t just more talk from politicians who are just like the old politicians they hated,” the memo reads.

The group isn’t alone in floating a new approach to the chamber’s 60-vote threshold for invoking cloture and ending debate. Some current and incoming lawmakers have signaled they would be open to going “nuclear” and abolishing the legislative filibuster, following in the footsteps of past majorities in both parties who have similarly changed how other Senate business is handled, like judicial nominees.

Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., argued in an op-ed in Time this week that Democrats could in some cases use existing tools, like a party-line process known as budget reconciliation, to pass their priorities. For non-budget items, like “bills to ban stock trading by federal officials or crack down on Trump’s corruption in plain sight,” Democrats should start by putting them up for a vote on the floor and letting “Republicans decide if they want to stay tied to such corruption.”

“And, if they do, then let’s clear away Senate filibuster rules and pass the laws anyway,” she wrote.

Sen.-elect James Talarico of Texas told NBC News on Monday ahead of his victory, “I believe the majority has the right to govern in this country, and the filibuster, which is not in the Constitution and is a Jim Crow relic, is leading to gridlock in our nation’s capital.”

Schumer ignored a question about nuking the filibuster at Wednesday’s event, saying instead, “The first thing we’ll do is reduce costs and work the legislative process.”

It would take 51 votes in the Senate for Democrats to go “nuclear” and establish a new precedent, a math problem that Thune knows all too well. The Republican from South Dakota faced loud criticism from conservatives online and hard-liners in his own party who said he wasn’t doing enough to try to pass a sprawling voter ID bill known as the SAVE America Act. But Thune repeatedly insisted he didn’t have the votes to change the chamber’s filibuster rules.

Many senators fear that blowing up the 60-vote threshold would fundamentally change the role of the Senate and its ability to tap the brakes on legislation that doesn’t have durable bipartisan support. Democrats went through a similar debate over the filibuster in 2022, during Schumer’s first stint as majority leader. Even as others in their party pushed to pass sweeping voting rights legislation by any means necessary, Kyrsten Sinema and Joe Manchin, then moderate Democratic senators, held firm against filibuster changes.

And Schumer will have at least one Democrat on his hands who has already spoken up in defense of the filibuster, reversing his previous position. “I now recognize how important the filibuster is and the state our country would be in without it,” Sen. John Fetterman of Pennsylvania wrote in a Washington Post op-ed earlier this summer, praising Sinema and Manchin as “courageous” holdouts who were wrongly “vilified” for holding the line.

None of that points to an easy ride for the incoming Democratic majority leader. And if fights over the filibuster weren’t enough, the caucus is also bracing for a different kind of intraparty angst, this time over impeachment.

The next likely House Judiciary Committee chairman, Rep. Jamie Raskin, D-Md., told reporters on Thursday: “I have not had a single member of Congress or even a candidate say we’ve got to start out by impeaching Donald Trump.”

“For us, impeachment is not a fetish. It’s certainly not a taboo. We’ve impeached the guy twice,” he said, referring to Democrats’ efforts during the first Trump administration. “The problem has been over on the Senate side, and for us, it is a tool in the constitutional toolkit.”

Even if the House were to impeach Trump, a conviction would require a two-thirds supermajority vote in the Senate. And some Democrats are eager not to waste their hard-earned political capital on an impeachment quest that could alienate moderate voters and do nothing to bring down costs for everyday Americans.

Illinois Gov. JB Pritzker, who boosted candidates in battleground races and recently released a roadmap for the new Congress, said at the CAP event on Thursday that lawmakers should “hold oversight hearings and expose the corruption of this administration. I’m not suggesting that that necessarily leads to impeachment. Maybe it does.”

Sounding more certain for Democrats in both chambers is their desire to impeach Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth over the war in Iran.

But that could be dealt with in the lame duck if Rep. Thomas Massie’s, R-Ky., resolution prevails next week. His proposal includes eight articles of impeachment, including allegations that Hegseth violated the 1973 War Powers Resolution in his prosecution of the Iran war and acted illegally in military campaigns in Venezuela, Yemen, the Caribbean Sea and the eastern Pacific Ocean.

Hegseth said in a video on Thursday of impeaching: “Didn’t work against President Trump. Won’t work with me. You can rest assured of that.”

Indivisible’s memo names impeaching Hegseth as a priority, as well as blocking a “business-as-usual” National Defense Authorization Act in the lame duck and refusing to fund the Department of Defense if “it doesn’t cut off money for the war.”

But it’s not clear that Democrats in Congress are ready to play that kind of hard ball. As they weigh their approach to the next era, they’re still half-stunned themselves by their larger than expected wins — and trying to put themselves in the best position ahead of the next elections in 2028.

GOP Rep. Christopher H. Smith of New Jersey told reporters on Thursday at the Capitol he hopes Democrats don’t hold up the NDAA “for the good of the country.”

“The NDAA is such an important bill for our national security, and it’s usually very bipartisan. I hope it is,” he said.

John Donnelly and Jacob Fulton contributed to this report.

Core Scientific to quit Bitcoin mining by year-end as AI business surges

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The former Bitcoin mining heavyweight plans to become a pure-play data center provider in 2027, with mining now accounting for just 7.5% of quarterly revenue.

Core Scientific said it expects to exit Bitcoin mining by the end of 2026 as it shifts its business toward artificial intelligence and high-performance computing infrastructure.

“We expect to complete the wind-down during the fourth quarter and enter 2027 as a pure-play, high-density colocation provider,” said Core Scientific chief financial officer Jim Nygaard during a Q3 earnings call on Thursday.

Core Scientific had previously disclosed in a July quarterly filing that hosted mining would end by Dec. 31 as it continued to wind down its self-mining operations. The comment establishes a targeted completion date for its complete Bitcoin mining exit.

Its latest quarterly results show how far the business has shifted. Third-quarter self-mining revenue fell to $12.3 million from $57.4 million a year earlier, while revenue from hosting other miners dropped to $5.2 million from $8.7 million.

Bitcoin mining generated just $17.5 million, or 7.5%, of Core Scientific’s $232.9 million in third-quarter revenue, compared with $215.4 million from colocation services.

The company had nearly 50% fewer miners online at the end of September than at the end of 2025, according to Nygaard.

Core Scientific’s move into AI hosting took shape in 2024 after signing an initial hosting agreement with CoreWeave in March that year, followed by a much larger deal announced in June.

At the time, Core Scientific presented AI hosting as an expansion alongside its mining business. Its June 2024 announcement emphasized preserving Bitcoin mining capacity.

By December 2025, The Wall Street Journal reported that Core Scientific was already targeting to wind down its Bitcoin mining operations by 2028.

Related: American Bitcoin’s $126M profit masks mining pressure as rivals turn to AI

Mining economics added pressure in the months following.

Core Scientific recorded $266.5 million in mining-related asset impairments in the first quarter of 2026, citing weaker Bitcoin prices, historically low hashprice and falling digital asset mining equipment values in the secondary market.

During the second quarter, it terminated its mining equipment agreement with Block and Proto, recording a $41.9 million loss as part of the transition.

The planned exit comes as the US election results introduce fresh uncertainty for companies expanding AI infrastructure.

Opponents of data-center development were among the winners of the Democrats’ sweeping midterm gains, Reuters reported Wednesday.

The backlash centers partly on electricity bills and the demands large facilities place on local resources.

Cointelegraph reached out to Core Scientific for comment.
